<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the Kollár–Pardon conjecture: the universal cover of a connected normal projective complex variety is biholomorphic to a semialgebraic open subset of a projective variety if and only if it is a product of a bounded symmetric domain, a complex affine space, and a simply connected normal projective variety.

- **claim-cover-classification** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $X$ be a connected normal projective variety over $\mathbb C$, and let $\widetilde X$ be its universal cover. The following conditions are equivalent:* 1. *$\widetilde X$ is biholomorphic to a semialgebraic open subset of a projective variety.* 2. *There are an integer $m\geq0$, a bounded symmetric domain $D$, and a simply connected normal projective variety $F$ such that $$\widetilde X\simeq D\times\mathbb C^m\times F.$$* *A point is allowed as either $D$ or $F$. No algebraicity of the deck transformations is assumed.
  - Explanation: A semialgebraic projective-open presentation of the ordinary universal cover is equivalent to the stated bounded symmetric, affine and simply connected normal projective product. Singular compact factors and nonalgebraic deck actions remain permitted.
- **claim-power-bounds-preparation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prep-puiseux)): Let $A$ be globally subanalytic and let $f(v,t)$ be globally subanalytic for $v\in A$ and $0<t<\epsilon(v)$, where $\epsilon$ is positive. If $f(v,t)>0$, there is an integer $N$ such that, for every fixed $v$, $$t^N<f(v,t)<t^{-N} \quad\hbox{for all sufficiently small }t>0.$$ The threshold may depend on $v$. For finitely many such functions one may use a common $N$.* *If $A$ is an open real box, then near a generic $v_0$ a given finite collection of functions admits convergent expansions $$f(v,t)=\sum_{j\geq j_0}a_j(v)t^{j/q}.$$ with a common positive integer $q$ and coefficients real analytic in $v$. After multiplication by a sufficiently high power of $t$, these are jointly real analytic functions of $(v,t^{1/q})$ through $t=0$. Convergence and every fixed number of $v$-derivatives are uniform on smaller compact parameter boxes.
  - Explanation: Positive globally subanalytic functions have common power exponents, while generic parameter restrictions yield joint convergent Puiseux expansions. Thresholds can depend on the fixed parameter.
- **claim-projective-models** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prep-projective-models)): Suppose an algebraic family in projective coordinates is pulled back by a local analytic parameter map. Suppose an irreducible member is specified by a marked irreducible germ. Near a generic point of a maximally totally real open parameter box, one may track that component on a local branch and replace the family and its evaluation maps by a smooth proper projective family of connected smooth models. The evaluation maps are holomorphic, and their restrictions to the projective fibres are algebraic of bounded data. Real analytic parameter dependence can first be complexified.
  - Explanation: A marked irreducible germ can be tracked and resolved into connected smooth projective models near generic totally real parameters, with holomorphic evaluation and bounded algebraic fibre data.
- **claim-kernel-pseudoconvexity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prep-kernel-pseudoconvexity)): An open kernel of locally pseudoconvex open sets is locally pseudoconvex. The assertion also holds in varying manifolds equipped with smoothly converging holomorphic coordinate charts.
  - Explanation: The neighborhood-based open-kernel operation preserves local pseudoconvexity, including in smoothly varying holomorphic charts.
- **claim-finite-jet-specialization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prep-finite-jet-specialization)): In bounded projective charts let a family of sets be described by finitely many Boolean combinations of equations and inequalities polynomial in the real fibre coordinates $y$, with coefficients real analytic in real parameter coordinates $b$. Fix $x$ and substitute $$b=x+R_t h,$$ where the entries of $R_t$ have convergent Puiseux expansions, $R_t\to0$, and both $R_t$ and $R_t^{-1}$ have power bounds. The upper limits of these sets, and hence the open kernels of their complements, are semialgebraic in $(h,y)$ on the central fibre. Here $h$ ranges over its whole affine space, while the substitution is tested only where $b$ belongs to the original parameter box. When external parameters are present, assume that all data, including $R_t$, are jointly globally subanalytic and that the displayed analytic coefficients are jointly analytic. Then the resulting descriptions have analytic coefficients after generic restriction. If all the original data are merely globally subanalytic, the corresponding conclusions are subanalytic.
  - Explanation: Polynomial fibre descriptions with analytic coefficients retain semialgebraic upper limits after the specified Puiseux normalization. Merely globally subanalytic data give the weaker subanalytic conclusion.
- **claim-generic-distance-convergence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prep-generic-distance-convergence)): Let $x$ range over an open real box $U$, let $K$ be compact and globally subanalytic, and let $d_t(x,z)$ be a bounded globally subanalytic family of functions on $K$, for $0<t<t_0$, uniformly Lipschitz in $z$. There is a relatively closed subanalytic subset $E\subset U$ of dimension smaller than $\dim U$ such that the extension $$\widehat d(x,z,t)= \begin{cases} d_t(x,z),&t>0,\\ d_0(x,z),&t=0, \end{cases} \qquad d_0(x,z)=\lim_{t\downarrow0}d_t(x,z)$$ is jointly continuous at every $(x,z,0)$ with $x\in U\setminus E$ and $z\in K$. In particular, for such $x$, if $x_t\to x$ and $z_t\to z$, then $$d_t(x_t,z_t)\longrightarrow d_0(x,z).$$ For every compact $L\subset U\setminus E$, the convergence $d_t\to d_0$ is uniform on $L\times K$. One common exceptional set and one smaller open center box may be used for finitely many such tests. For countably many tests one may exclude the union of their exceptional sets, but no common open center box is asserted. These conclusions apply in particular to bounded, capped distance functions of definable closed sets.
  - Explanation: Uniformly Lipschitz definable distance tests extend continuously at generic real centers. Finite collections admit a common good neighborhood; countable collections do not acquire that neighborhood conclusion.
- **claim-freezing-real-center** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prep-generic-freezing)): Let a definable family be written using $\operatorname{Re}b$ and $\operatorname{Im}b$, and let $R_t\to0$ be a fixed real power matrix, independent of the real center $x$. For the substitution $$b=x+R_t(u+iv)$$ its upper-limit and open-kernel tests at generic $x$ are independent of $u$. Thus the kernels are invariant under real translations in the scaled coordinates. The tests may include remaining bounded fibre variables jointly. More generally, when a normalization $R_t(x)$ depends definably on $x$, Lemma <a href="#prep:generic-distance-convergence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:generic-distance-convergence">9</a> applies to the normalized closed-set tests themselves.
  - Explanation: For a center-independent real power matrix, bounded real shifts disappear from generic limit tests. Center-dependent normalizations require testing the normalized closed sets themselves.
- **claim-power-diagonalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prep-power-diagonalization)): Let $f(e,t,v)$ be a bounded globally subanalytic function with iterated limit $f_{00}(v)=\lim_{t\downarrow0}\lim_{e\downarrow0} f(e,t,v)$. There is an integer $N_0$ such that for every integer $N>N_0$, $$\lim_{t\downarrow0}f(t^N,t,v)=f_{00}(v)$$ for each fixed $v$. The same choice works for finitely many tests; the parameter $v$ may include fixed box sizes and positive accuracies. For bounded closed-set tests, apply this to the distance functions on slightly larger boxes, retaining a smaller box to avoid artificial edge effects.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently high power diagonal preserves the iterated definable limit for each fixed auxiliary parameter, with a common exponent for finitely many tests.
- **claim-polynomial-hole-maximum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-holes-polynomial-maximum)): Let $B$ be a complex manifold and let $J\subset B\times\mathbb C$ be closed. Assume that $J\to B$ is proper, its fibres are locally bounded in the line coordinate, and $(B\times\mathbb C)\setminus J$ is locally pseudoconvex. If $g(b,t)$ is monic of positive degree in $t$ with holomorphic coefficients in $b$, then $$\psi_g(b)=\log\max_{t\in J_b}\|g(b,t)\|$$ is plurisubharmonic, with value $-\infty$ for an empty fibre or a zero maximum. The assertion also holds for polynomials with nowhere-zero holomorphic leading coefficient, and for the constant test $1$.
  - Explanation: The logarithm of the polynomial maximum over properly varying holes is plurisubharmonic. Empty fibres and zero maxima receive the specified extended value.
- **claim-boundary-hole-trapping** (theorem, [source](#stmt-holes-boundary-trapping)): Let a proper holomorphic submersion be given near a relatively compact parameter interval $I$ on the boundary of a one-sided complex disc. Let $G$ be locally pseudoconvex on that side, and suppose every limit of its fibre holes as the transverse parameter tends to $I$ belongs to a relative divisor $D$ that extends holomorphically across $I$ and is proper in each fibre. Then, after restriction near an interior point of $I$, the holes on that side belong to $D$. The conclusion holds with additional fixed parameters whenever these hypotheses hold locally uniformly in those parameters.
  - Explanation: A divisor that contains all boundary limits of holes traps the nearby one-sided holes when it extends holomorphically and remains proper fibrewise.
- **claim-interior-hole-trapping** (theorem, [source](#stmt-holes-interior-trapping)): Let $G$ be open and locally pseudoconvex over a whole connected base box, with the preceding local confinement hypotheses. Suppose a relative divisor $D$ is holomorphic in a whole base box and contains the holes on a nonpluripolar parameter slice inside the box. Then it contains the holes nearby. In particular this applies to an open piece of a real analytic maximally totally real slice.
  - Explanation: Containment of locally confined holes on a nonpluripolar slice extends nearby in the connected base box.
- **claim-tangency-propagation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-holes-tangency-propagation)): Let $q:Q\to\Delta$ be a holomorphic submersion, let $G\subset Q$ be open and locally pseudoconvex, and let $A$ be a divisor in $Q_0$. Suppose $Q_0\setminus A$ is connected. Assume that on one side of a regular embedded real analytic arc through $0$, every limit of holes belongs to $A$. If $G_0$ is nonempty, then $$Q_0\setminus A\subset G_0.$$ It suffices to have these hypotheses on neighborhoods of compact paths in $Q_0\setminus A$.
  - Explanation: One-sided confinement and a nonempty central open fibre propagate membership along the connected complement of the central divisor.
- **claim-thin-hole-analyticity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-holes-thin-analyticity)): Let $M$ be a complex manifold of dimension $n$, and let $A\subset M$ be closed and subanalytic, with real dimension at most $2n-2$. If $M\setminus A$ is locally pseudoconvex, then $A$ is either empty or an analytic set of pure complex codimension one.
  - Explanation: A closed subanalytic omission of the stated real dimension with locally pseudoconvex complement is empty or a pure analytic divisor.
- **claim-relative-divisor-container** (theorem, [source](#stmt-holes-relative-divisor-container)): Let $q:Q\to B$ be a smooth projective family with connected fibres, and let $A\subset Q$ be a relative algebraic subset with analytic coefficient data, proper in every fibre. Near a generic parameter there is a relative effective divisor $D$ containing $A$, defined over the full smaller parameter box and proper in every fibre.
  - Explanation: A fibrewise proper relative algebraic subset admits a containing effective divisor over a full smaller box near a generic parameter.
- **claim-bounded-containers** (bound, [source](#stmt-holes-bounded-containers)): Consider a smooth proper projective family with connected fibres, and a bounded-data semialgebraic family of subsets of those fibres. There is a degree bound, depending only on the family description, such that every member contained in a proper complex algebraic subset is contained in an effective divisor of that degree or smaller. The parameters admitting such a container form a subanalytic set, and containers can be selected subanalytically. For a one-sided family approaching a generic real analytic parameter wall, its limiting containers can be chosen as proper effective divisors varying real analytically on that wall.
  - Explanation: A fixed bounded semialgebraic family description controls container degrees and permits subanalytic selection. Generic wall limits admit the stated real analytic divisor variation.
- **claim-projective-hole-propagation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-holes-projective-propagation)): Let $q:Q\to B$ be smooth, proper, and projective, with connected smooth fibres. Let $G\subset Q$ be open, subanalytic, locally pseudoconvex, and fibrewise semialgebraic with bounded data. Put $S=q(G)$, and let $S_0$ be a connected component of $S$. If $G_b$ contains a Zariski open dense subset of $Q_b$ for every $b$ in some nonempty open subset of $S_0$, then $$J=q^{-1}(S_0)\setminus G$$ is an analytic divisor, possibly empty. Its intersection with each fibre is proper, so every $G_b$, $b\in S_0$, contains a Zariski open dense subset of $Q_b$.
  - Explanation: Having a Zariski open dense fibre portion on an open parameter region propagates across the connected projection component, with analytic and fibrewise proper holes.
- **claim-fixed-container-propagation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-holes-container-propagation)): In Proposition <a href="#holes:projective-propagation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="holes:projective-propagation">19</a>, let $D$ be a relative analytic divisor defined on the full parameter box and proper in every fibre. If $J\subset D$ over a nonempty open subregion of $S_0$, then $J\subset D$ over all of $S_0$. The same conclusion holds for any relative proper analytic subset in place of $D$.
  - Explanation: An existing full-box relative proper analytic container that contains the holes on an open region contains them throughout the connected projection component.
- **claim-section-projection-test** (theorem, [source](#stmt-holes-section-test)): Suppose a full-box container $D$ as in Corollary <a href="#holes:container-propagation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="holes:container-propagation">20</a> is available. A local holomorphic section $\sigma$ of $q$ avoiding $D$ lies in $G$ at every parameter of $S_0$. Near a boundary point of $S_0$ that lies in the parameter box, $S_0$ is locally pseudoconvex wherever such a section exists.
  - Explanation: A holomorphic section avoiding the full-box container detects the projection domain and proves its local pseudoconvexity at suitable parameter boundary points.
- **claim-accessible-upper-containment** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-theorem)): Under these hypotheses, at generic $x$, for every finite $L>\|p\|$ and every $\eta>0$ there is $t_0=t_0(x,L,\eta)>0$ such that $$\label{loc:upper} 0<t<t_0\quad\Longrightarrow\quad A_t^x(L)\subset\{Z:\operatorname{dist}(Z,\overline{P_x})<\eta\}.$$ Consequently, if $t_n\downarrow0$ and paths in $D_{t_n}^x$ start at $p$, stay in one fixed bounded box, and have endpoints tending to $z$, then $z\in\overline{P_x}$. The same holds for starting points tending to $p$ inside a uniform initial ball. Fixed auxiliary parameters are permitted; generic restrictions are made in the real centers used for the recenterings.
  - Explanation: Triangular recentering control upgrades kernel inclusion to an outer limit statement for the seed-accessible components in each fixed bounded box. Moving starts are allowed inside the uniform initial ball.
- **claim-analytic-power-parametrization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-puiseux)): Let $U\subset\mathbb R^k$ be open and let $f:U\times(0,\epsilon)\to\mathbb R^m$ be bounded and globally subanalytic. Outside a closed subanalytic subset of $U$ of dimension less than $k$, locally in $x$, there are an integer $q\geq1$ and a real-analytic map $a(x,s)$, defined near $U'\times\{0\}$, such that $$f(x,t)=a(x,t^{1/q})\qquad(0<t<\epsilon').$$ Here $U'$ and $\epsilon'$ may be reduced. If a scalar coordinate of $f$ is positive, it either has a nonzero limit or, after a further generic restriction, has the form $$t^{\nu/q}a_0(x,t^{1/q}),\qquad a_0(x,0)>0, \quad \nu\in\mathbb N.$$ On smaller parameter boxes the analytic maps have a common complex neighborhood and all their derivatives are bounded.
  - Explanation: A bounded globally subanalytic family becomes jointly analytic after a common root substitution near generic real centers. Positive coordinates have the stated leading-unit factorization.
- **claim-controlled-replacement-paths** (theorem, [source](#stmt-loc-paths)): Let $U\subset\mathbb R^k$ be open, let $D\subset U\times(0,\epsilon)\times\mathbb R^n$ be a bounded globally subanalytic family of open sets, and let $v,w$ be bounded subanalytic sections that belong to the same component of $D_{x,t}$. Locally at generic $x_0\in U$, there are a neighborhood $U'$ of $x_0$, $\epsilon'>0$, and paths $$F_t:U'\times[0,1]\longrightarrow\mathbb R^n$$ with endpoints $v(x,t),w(x,t)$ and image in $D_{x,t}$, with the following uniform properties. For each $t$, $F_t(\cdot,a)$ extends holomorphically to one fixed complex neighborhood $U'_{\mathbb C}$, independently of $t$ and $a$. On a smaller such neighborhood there is $C<\infty$ such that $$\label{loc:path-estimate} \sup_{0<t<\epsilon'}\sup_{x\in U'_{\mathbb C}} \bigl(\|F_t(x,a)\|+\|F_t(x,a)-F_t(x,b)\|/\|a-b\|\bigr)\leq C \quad(a\ne b).$$ In particular, every fixed order of $x$-derivatives satisfies the same estimate, with its own constant. The replacement paths and their timing need not be subanalytic.
  - Explanation: Sections in the same definable component can be joined by replacement paths with a common holomorphic center neighborhood and uniform path regularity. Their timing need not be subanalytic.
- **claim-accessible-semidefinite-tests** (bound, [source](#stmt-loc-matrix-outer)): Suppose, in addition, that the marked kernel is described recursively by strict positive-definiteness tests for finitely many continuous Hermitian matrix functions $M_j(Z)$, and that every point of $\overline P$ satisfies $M_j(Z)\geq0$. Then for each finite $L$, at a generic fixed center, $$\inf_{Z\in A_t(L)}\lambda_{\min}(M_j(Z))\geq-o(1) \qquad(t\downarrow0)$$ simultaneously for all $j$. The assertion is uniform over the accessible component in that fixed box.
  - Explanation: Continuous Hermitian tests that are nonnegative on the kernel closure have asymptotically nonnegative least eigenvalues throughout each fixed-box accessible component.
- **claim-algebraic-null-plaques** (theorem, [source](#stmt-win-plaques)): Let $M$ be a regular patch of a real algebraic hypersurface in a smooth complex algebraic $n$-fold. Suppose one side is locally pseudoconvex and the Levi form has constant rank $r$ on $M$. Put $\ell=n-r-1$. The Levi-null distribution integrates into complex $\ell$-dimensional plaques. Each sufficiently small plaque is open in an irreducible complex algebraic variety of dimension $\ell$. Locally at generic transverse parameters these varieties form a real analytic family of bounded degree. After complexifying the parameters, taking the relevant components, and resolving, they have smooth projective models in a smooth proper family.
  - Explanation: Constant Levi rank on a pseudoconvex side yields complex null plaques lying openly in irreducible algebraic varieties. Generic transverse parameters admit bounded-degree smooth projective models.
- **claim-period-matrix** (theorem, [source](#stmt-win-period)): In the situation of Lemma <a href="#win:plaques" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="win:plaques">26</a>, there is a real analytic $(r+1)$-parameter family of plaques, with coordinates $(s,w)\in \mathbb R\times\mathbb R^r$, whose complexification evaluates with full complex rank near the original plaque. The remaining $r$ real transverse coordinates can be retained as external parameters. At a fixed real center $x_1$, let $L$ be the smooth projective model of its plaque. There is a symmetric meromorphic $r\times r$ matrix $H$ on $L$, holomorphic near the original plaque, such that $\Im H>0$ there and the weighted tangent inequality is $$\label{win:quadratic-test} q_z(s,w)=\Im s-(\Im w)^{\mathsf T} (\Im H(z))^{-1}\Im w>0.$$ The meromorphic data depend analytically on the generic external parameters.
  - Explanation: A contact-compatible plaque family produces a symmetric meromorphic period matrix whose positive imaginary part determines the weighted quadratic tangent domain.
- **claim-whole-seed-bergman-fibre** (theorem, [source](#stmt-win-seed-bergman)): At every $z_0$ in a sufficiently small original plaque patch, the component of the kernel fibre containing $(i,0)$ is precisely $$T_A=\{(s,w)\in\mathbb C^{r+1}:\Im s>(\Im w)^{\mathsf T}A\Im w\}, \qquad A=(\Im H(z_0))^{-1}>0.$$ Its Bergman kernel is strictly positive at every point. This is a statement about the entire fibre component, not a relatively compact part of it.
  - Explanation: The entire marked seed fibre is the specified positive quadratic tube, and its Bergman kernel is positive everywhere. The assertion is not restricted to truncated slices.
- **claim-exact-first-epigraph** (theorem, [source](#stmt-win-first-epigraph)): There is a connected semialgebraic locally pseudoconvex domain $V_2\subset L$ such that $H$ is holomorphic on $V_2$, $\Im H>0$ there, and $$\label{win:epigraph} E=\{(s,w,z):z\in V_2,\quad \Im s>(\Im w)^{\mathsf T}(\Im H(z))^{-1}\Im w\}.$$ In particular $V_2=\{z:((i,0),z)\in E\}$.
  - Explanation: The whole selected kernel component is exactly the positive quadratic epigraph over a connected semialgebraic locally pseudoconvex plaque-model domain.
- **claim-finite-taylor-extraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-win-finite-taylor)): Suppose $A_t(x)$ and $A_t(x)^{-1}$ have joint Puiseux expansions on a real parameter neighborhood, with polynomial bounds in $t^{-1}$, and $R_t(x)$ has the same property and tends to zero locally uniformly. If $f$ is holomorphic on a fixed complex neighborhood, then any expression obtained by multiplying $f(x+R_tz)$ on either side by such Puiseux matrices, and subtracting a similarly bounded constant term, has a polynomial holomorphic limit on every bounded $z$-box whenever it is locally bounded on one nonempty complex open $z$-set. The limit and convergence are analytic in a generic real center and smooth to all orders in $z$ on bounded boxes.* *In addition, if $R_t$ is invertible and $$C_t(x,u)=R_t(x)^{-1}R_t(x+R_t(x)u)$$ is bounded on real bounded $u$-boxes, it converges smoothly on those boxes to a polynomial $G_x(u)$. After generic restriction, $\det G_x(u)=1$, and the limiting matrices have polynomial inverses.
  - Explanation: Joint Puiseux control and local boundedness remove negative Laurent terms, yielding polynomial holomorphic limits on bounded boxes. Bounded recentering ratios acquire polynomial inverses after generic restriction.
- **claim-siegel-polynomial-normalization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-win-siegel-normalization)): Let $S_2$ be a fixed parameter domain with normalization $R_t^2(x_2)$, open-kernel component $P_2$, and a fixed interior seed $p_2$. Let $H_*$ be symmetric and holomorphic on a full neighborhood of $x_2$, with $\Im H_*>0$ on $S_2$. Assume the normalizations have joint Puiseux expansions and polynomial inverse bounds. Define $$\begin{aligned} T_t&=\bigl(\Im H_*(x_2+R_t^2p_2)\bigr)^{1/2},\label{win:T}\\ H_t(z)&=T_t^{-1}\bigl(H_*(x_2+R_t^2z) -\Re H_*(x_2+R_t^2p_2)\bigr)T_t^{-1}.\label{win:Ht} \end{aligned}$$ Then $H_t$ converges on all bounded complex boxes to a symmetric holomorphic polynomial matrix $\tau$, with $\tau(p_2)=iI$ and $\Im\tau>0$ on $P_2$.
  - Explanation: Seed normalization of the varying positive period matrix converges on all bounded complex boxes to a symmetric holomorphic polynomial matrix, strictly positive over the lower marked kernel.
- **claim-projective-windows** (construction, [source](#stmt-win-construction)): Let $Y$ be a connected smooth projective variety and let $V\subset Y$ be a nonempty connected semialgebraic open subset that is locally pseudoconvex at its boundary. The following data exist, locally at generic centers in totally real parameter boxes. The same assertion holds in smooth projective families with additional real parameters, analytic coefficients, and bounded algebraic fibre data, when the hypotheses hold fibrewise. The constructed data depend analytically on those parameters after generic restriction and may be complexified locally.* *There is a complex box $B\subset\mathbb C^d$, a smooth proper projective family $q:Q\to B$ with connected fibres of dimension $\dim Y-d$, and a holomorphic evaluation $\mathrm{ev}:Q\to Y$, algebraic with bounded data in the projective variables and of full rank on a coordinate patch meeting every fibre. Its fibre images $Z_b=\mathrm{ev}(Q_b)$ are irreducible projective varieties of dimension $\dim Y-d$, and $Q_b\to Z_b$ is proper and surjective. If $G=\mathrm{ev}^{-1}(V)$, a component $S$ of $q(G)$ and a relative proper algebraic subset $D\subset Q$ satisfy $$\label{win:container} Q_b\setminus D_b\subset G_b\qquad(b\in S).$$ The container $D$ is analytic on the full box and proper in every fibre, after shrinking the box. The actual holes $Q\|_S\setminus G$ are analytic; their images give precisely $Z_b\setminus V$ and are algebraic in each fibre. The domain $S$ is locally pseudoconvex at its parameter boundary. Its boundary inside $B$ is disjoint from $q(G)$. All stated shrinkings of $B$ may be made before the choice of scales and sequences.* *There are a fixed interior seed $p$, real block diagonal matrices $R_t(x)$ tending to zero with polynomial inverse bounds, and a component $P$ of the open kernel of $$S_{t,x}=R_t(x)^{-1}(S-x)$$ at $p$. A fixed neighborhood of $p$ is eventually in $S_{t,x}$. The domain $P$ is a finite recursive tower, beginning at a point and adding a block $(s,w)\in\mathbb C\times\mathbb C^r$ over a previously constructed domain $P_2$ by the condition $$\label{win:tower} z\in P_2,\qquad \Im M(s,w,z)>0,\qquad M(s,w,z)=\begin{pmatrix}s&w^{\mathsf T}\\w&\tau(z)\end{pmatrix},$$ where $\tau$ is symmetric and polynomial holomorphic and $\Im\tau>0$ on $P_2$.* *For each bounded real $u$-box the ratios $$\label{win:ratio} R_t(x)^{-1}R_t(x+R_t(x)u)\longrightarrow G_x(u)$$ converge with every derivative. The matrix $G_x$ is polynomial and block diagonal, each block depends only on later block coordinates, $G_x(0)=I$, and every block has determinant one. Its inverse is polynomial. For all sufficiently small real $u$, $$\label{win:orbit} Z\longmapsto u+G_x(u)Z$$ is an automorphism of $P$.* *Finally the bounded-path localization conclusion holds: any limit of endpoints of paths in $S_{t,x}$ starting at the seed and staying in a fixed bounded box belongs to $\overline P$. Equivalently, on each fixed bounded box the accessible components have outer distance to $\overline P$ tending to zero.
  - Explanation: Generic boundary scaling constructs full projective windows with analytic holes inside a full-box container and a recursive polynomial matrix tower. The same fixed parameter domain supports triangular recenterings and bounded-path localization.
- **claim-tower-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-win-tower-properties)): The domain $P$ of Theorem <a href="#win:construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="win:construction">32</a> is diffeomorphic to $\mathbb R^{2d}$, where $d=\dim_{\mathbb C}P$. In particular it is contractible. It is Stein and biholomorphic to a bounded domain of the same complex dimension. Complete real holomorphic vector fields from the identity component of its automorphism group span every complex tangent space over $\mathbb C$.
  - Explanation: The recursive tower is Euclidean up to diffeomorphism, contractible and Stein, with a bounded realization of the same complex dimension. Complete real holomorphic vector fields span its complex tangent spaces.
- **claim-resolved-cover-pseudoconvexity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quo-pseudoconvex)): The open set $V$ is locally pseudoconvex in $Y$.
  - Explanation: The smooth resolved covering open set is locally pseudoconvex in its smooth projective ambient variety.
- **claim-tower-proper-distance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quo-tower)): The domain $P$ is diffeomorphic to $\mathbb R^{2d}$, is Stein, and is biholomorphic to a bounded domain in $\mathbb C^d$. Its Kobayashi distance is a proper distance inducing its usual topology.
  - Explanation: Besides its bounded Stein realization and Euclidean topology, the tower has a proper Kobayashi distance compatible with its ordinary topology.
- **claim-decaying-guards** (construction, [source](#stmt-quo-guard)): There are normalized accessible parameter regions $A_i\subset\{\|Z\|<R_i\}$, where $R_i\to\infty$, and holomorphic functions $h$ on their neighbourhoods, given by the same formula $$\label{quo:guard-formula} h(Z)=\exp\left(-\alpha\sum_j\operatorname{tr} \log(I-iM_j(Z))\right),$$ with the following properties. Their values lie in a fixed sector $\{\zeta:\|\arg\zeta\|<\theta\}$, $\theta<\pi/2$, and are uniformly bounded. On $A_i$, $\|h(Z)\|\leq C(1+\|Z\|)^{-\alpha}$. Every compact subset of $P$ is eventually contained in $A_i$. There are connected open subsets $H_i\subset A_i$, containing the seed and eventually every compact subset of $P$, such that $$u_i=\log\|h\|+a_i\|Z\|^2>m_i\quad\hbox{on }H_i, \qquad a_i>0,$$ where $a_iR_i^2\leq1$, $m_i\to-\infty$, and every boundary of $H_i$ admitting an incidence with a point of $V$ has $\|h\|\leq\delta_i$, with $\delta_i\to0$.
  - Explanation: Common holomorphic guards have sector bounds and decay on expanding accessible regions. Their high regions exhaust compact parts of the tower and force small guard values at incidence-admitting boundaries.
- **claim-intrinsic-window-fibres** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quo-incidence)): The union $U_i=\bigcup_{b\in H_i}(Z_b\cap V)$ is open in $V$. Distinct fibres in this union are disjoint, and there is a holomorphic submersion $$\pi_i:U_i\longrightarrow H_i, \qquad \pi_i^{-1}(b)=Z_b\cap V.$$ These fibres are connected. Moreover, if $T$ is a smooth connected projective variety, $D\subset T$ is a proper analytic subset, and $g:T\setminus D\to V$ is holomorphic, then any such image meeting a fibre of $\pi_i$ is contained in that fibre.
  - Explanation: High window fibres form disjoint connected leaves of a holomorphic submersion. Holomorphic images of smooth projective varieties minus analytic subsets cannot cross such a leaf once they meet it.
- **claim-cutoff-guard-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quo-cutoff-normal)): Let $D$ be a complex manifold, let $A_i\subset D$ be open, and let $h_i\in\mathcal O(A_i)$ satisfy $\|h_i\|\leq C$. Suppose that, for every $x\in\partial A_i\cap D$, $$\limsup_{A_i\ni z\to x}\|h_i(z)\|\leq\epsilon_i, \qquad \epsilon_i\longrightarrow0.$$ Extend $h_i$ by zero outside $A_i$. These extended functions have a subsequence converging uniformly on compact subsets of $D$ to a holomorphic function. Suppose in addition that their nonzero values lie in $\|\arg\zeta\|\leq\theta<\pi/2$, that $D$ is connected, and that $x_i\to x$, $h_i(x_i)\to a\ne0$. Then every resulting limit is nowhere zero, and every compact subset of $D$ lies in $A_i$ eventually.
  - Explanation: Uniformly bounded guards with uniformly vanishing internal boundary values have holomorphic compact limits after zero extension. Sector control and a nonzero moving-point limit force eventual compact containment.
- **claim-projection-chart-trapping** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quo-trapping)): Let $D_i$ exhaust a connected complex manifold $D$, and let $g_i:D_i\to V$ be holomorphic. Suppose that at points $x_i\to x$ the images belong to $U_i$, and their normalized parameters $\pi_i(g_i(x_i))$ converge to the seed $p\in P$. Then every compact subset of $D$ is eventually mapped into $U_i$; after taking a subsequence, $\pi_i\circ g_i$ converges normally on $D$ to a holomorphic map into $P$. The same assertion holds for maps into a manifold equipped with these projection charts and their guards.
  - Explanation: A moving point whose normalized parameter approaches the seed forces eventual chart containment on all compact subsets and normal convergence of projected maps into the strict tower.
- **claim-inverse-chart-tightness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quo-inverse-tightness)): For every compact $K\subset P$, the images $\psi_i(K)$ eventually lie in a compact subset of one fixed chart $M_j$.
  - Explanation: Inverse images of compact tower sets stay in a compact subset of a single fixed quotient chart, enabling a genuine inverse limit map.
- **claim-normal-source-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quo-descent)): The map $f$ descends uniquely to an equivariant holomorphic map $f_X:\widetilde X\to M$. Its fibres are connected and, locally on $M$, have simultaneous projective compactifications with analytic holes. Each fibre is biholomorphic to a semialgebraic open subset of a projective variety.
  - Explanation: The quotient map descends uniquely through the normal modification, retaining equivariance, connected fibres and local simultaneous projective compactifications with analytic holes.
- **claim-intrinsic-projection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-quo-projection)): Under the hypotheses of condition <span class="upright">(i)</span> of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> and the boundary construction of Theorem <a href="#win:construction" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="win:construction">32</a>, there are a contractible Stein manifold $M$, biholomorphic to the boundedly realizable tower $P$, an equivariant surjective submersion $f:V\to M$, and an equivariant holomorphic map $f_X:\widetilde X\to M$ with $f=f_X\mu$. Both maps have connected fibres. Their fibres have simultaneous projective compactifications with analytic holes locally on $M$, after a deck translation. The normal-source fibres are normal and semialgebraically compactifiable up to biholomorphism, and $f_X$ is flat.* *Suppose additionally that the image $\Lambda$ of the deck action on $M$ is discrete and acts freely. Then the descended proper map $X\to M/\Lambda$, its pullback to $M$, and every connected finite covering family of that pullback are flat and topologically locally trivial, with connected normal projective fibres. Their induced resolution families are smooth proper projective families; the resolution maps are proper, have connected point fibres, and are bimeromorphic on every fibre. The induced maps on integral degree-one cohomology are morphisms of local systems.
  - Explanation: The constructed intrinsic quotient is the boundedly realizable contractible Stein tower. The normal-source map is flat; discreteness and freeness additionally yield proper topologically locally trivial normal projective families and compatible resolution cohomology systems.
- **claim-abelian-cover-liouville** (theorem, [source](#stmt-grp-liouville)): Let $a:W\to Z$ be a connected regular covering of a compact connected Kähler manifold. If its deck group is virtually abelian, every bounded holomorphic function on $W$ is constant.
  - Explanation: Every bounded holomorphic function on a connected virtually abelian regular cover of a compact connected Kähler manifold is constant.
- **claim-countable-level-local-finiteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-grp-finite-level-projection)): Let $q:Q\to B$ be a proper holomorphic map from a complex manifold, let $h:Q\dashrightarrow C$ be a meromorphic map to a projective variety, and let $G_0\subset Q$ be open with $h\|_{G_0}$ holomorphic. Fix $s\in C$. If $$q\bigl(G_0\cap h^{-1}(s)\bigr)$$ is countable, it is locally finite in $B$.
  - Explanation: Properness of the full family and meromorphic extension make a countable attained level projection locally finite, even though the valid locus is only open.
- **claim-core-level-sets** (theorem, [source](#stmt-grp-countable-core-level)): For every such $s$, the set $D_s$ is countable and locally finite in $M$. The union of these sets, as $s$ runs through very general values, is dense in $M$.
  - Explanation: Very general compact special core fibres produce countable locally finite transverse images whose union is dense in the intrinsic quotient.
- **claim-transverse-discreteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-grp-discrete)): The image $\Lambda$ is discrete in $\operatorname{Aut}(M)$ and acts cocompactly on $M$.
  - Explanation: Invariance of the dense collection of locally finite core levels forces the transverse image to be discrete. Equivariance and compactness of the source quotient give cocompactness.
- **claim-free-transverse-finite-cover** (theorem, [source](#stmt-grp-free-finite-index)): There is a torsion-free finite-index subgroup $\Lambda_1\subset\Lambda$ acting freely on $M$. Its inverse image in $\Gamma$ corresponds to a connected finite cover of the original normal projective variety $X$.
  - Explanation: A finite-index transverse subgroup acts freely and is torsion-free. Its inverse image gives a connected finite cover of the original normal projective variety.
- **claim-transverse-symmetry** (classification, [source](#stmt-grp-discrete-symmetric)): Under the intrinsic-projection hypotheses recalled above, the transverse image is discrete and cocompact. After a finite cover it acts freely, and $M$ is biholomorphic to a bounded symmetric domain, with a point allowed.
  - Explanation: Under the intrinsic-projection assumptions, the transverse action becomes free after a finite cover and the bounded base is symmetric. The point case is included.
- **claim-compactifiable-cover-abelianity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-grp-compactifiable-cover)): Let $a:U\to N$ be a connected regular covering of a smooth connected projective manifold, with deck group $K$. Suppose $U$ is a Zariski open subset, in the analytic sense, of a smooth projective manifold $\overline U$. Then, using <a href="#grp:abelianity-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="grp:abelianity-input">[grp:abelianity-input]</a>, the group $K$ is virtually abelian.
  - Explanation: A regular cover of a smooth projective manifold that is analytically Zariski open in a smooth projective compactification has virtually abelian deck group, using compact special abelianity.
- **claim-fibre-kernel-abelianity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-grp-kernel-abelian)): After the finite-cover replacement of Lemma <a href="#grp:free-finite-index" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="grp:free-finite-index">47</a>, let $K=\ker(\Gamma\to\Lambda)$. The group $K$ is finitely generated and virtually abelian. For each $m\in M$, it acts on $f^{-1}(m)$ as the deck group of a connected regular covering of a smooth projective fibre.
  - Explanation: After the free-action finite cover, the transverse kernel is finitely generated and virtually abelian. It is the deck group of each connected regular fibre cover.
- **claim-characteristic-fibre-cover** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cmp-characteristic)): There is a characteristic finite-index subgroup $K'\subset K$ which is free abelian. The quotient $\mathcal X=U/K'$ is normal and its map $p:\mathcal X\to M$ is proper and flat. It is topologically locally trivial with connected normal projective fibres $N_m$, and $$\pi_1(N_m)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ }\pi_1(\mathcal X)=K'.$$ The restriction of $U\to\mathcal X$ to $N_m$ is its connected universal cover.
  - Explanation: A characteristic finite-index free abelian subgroup produces a proper flat normal covering family over the contractible base. Its fibres have the same fundamental group as its total space and lift to connected universal covers.
- **claim-relative-albanese-descent** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cmp-albanese)): There is an integer $a\geq0$ with $K'\simeq\mathbb Z^{2a}$ and a smooth proper family of Albanese torsors $q:\mathcal A\to M$ and a proper morphism $$\alpha:\mathcal X\longrightarrow\mathcal A$$ over $M$ whose restriction to $N_m$ is its Albanese morphism. Both constructions are equivariant for $\Gamma/K'$. The morphisms $\alpha\|_{N_m}$ and $\alpha$ induce isomorphisms on fundamental groups.
  - Explanation: The normal fibres admit an equivariant relative Albanese torsor family and proper Albanese morphism, with integral fundamental-group isomorphisms and even free-abelian rank.
- **claim-proper-affine-base** (construction, [source](#stmt-cmp-affine-base)): There is a $\Gamma$-equivariant proper flat surjection $$j:U\longrightarrow T, \qquad T\simeq M\times\mathbb C^a,$$ whose fibres are connected, simply connected, normal projective varieties. The action of $\Gamma$ on $T$ is proper and cocompact and has finite stabilizers. A polarization of the finite cover of $X$ pulls back to a $\Gamma$-invariant line bundle $L$ on $U$, ample on each fibre of $j$.
  - Explanation: The entire Albanese pullback yields an equivariant proper flat map to a product of the symmetric base and affine space, with simply connected normal projective fibres and a fibrewise ample invariant line bundle.
- **claim-invariant-closed-set-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cmp-closed-rigidity)): Let a countable discrete group act properly and cocompactly by diffeomorphisms on a contractible smooth manifold $T$ homeomorphic to $\mathbb R^b$. If a nonempty closed invariant subset $A\subset T$ has the homotopy type of a finite CW complex, then $A=T$.
  - Explanation: A nonempty closed invariant subset of finite CW homotopy type must fill the Euclidean contractible manifold under the stated proper cocompact smooth action.
- **claim-semialgebraic-chow-base** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cmp-chow-base)): Fix a semialgebraic realization $U\subset Z$, where $Z$ is projective, and a projective embedding of $Z$. Let $n$ be the fibre dimension of $j$ and $d$ the degree of one fibre. In the projective Chow space $\operatorname{Chow}_{n,d}(Z)$, the locus $$C=\{c:\ c\text{ is an integral cycle and }\|c\|\subset U\}$$ is semialgebraic, and the map $t\mapsto[j^{-1}(t)]$ is a homeomorphism from $T$ onto $C$.
  - Explanation: The affine base is homeomorphic to the semialgebraic fixed-degree locus of integral cycles supported entirely in the given cover presentation.
- **claim-polarized-isomorphism-loci** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cmp-polarized-locus)): For every fixed polarized fibre $(F,L_F)$ of $j$, the locus of fibres polarized-isomorphic to it is semialgebraic in $C$.
  - Explanation: Each fixed polarized fibre determines a semialgebraic isomorphism locus, using bounded graph degree and integral marking without assuming that the polarization extends to the compactification.
- **claim-global-compact-product** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cmp-product)): There is a simply connected normal projective variety $F$ such that $$\widetilde X\simeq M\times\mathbb C^a\times F.$$
  - Explanation: Rigidity of invariant semialgebraic fibre classes forces polarized isotriviality. Local Hilbert products and the Oka principle give the global product with a simply connected normal projective fibre.
- **claim-product-presentation-converse** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cmp-converse)): If $\widetilde X\simeq D\times\mathbb C^m\times F$, with $D$ a bounded symmetric domain and $F$ projective, then $\widetilde X$ is biholomorphic to a semialgebraic open subset of a projective variety.
  - Explanation: The compact dual realization of the bounded symmetric factor and the ordinary affine projective chart provide a semialgebraic projective-open presentation of the product.
- **claim-remmert-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-remmert-semialgebraic)): Let $X$ be a connected normal projective complex variety whose ordinary universal cover $U$ has a semialgebraic projective-open presentation. Write $U\simeq B\times F$, where $B=D\times\mathbb C^m$, as in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>. Then $U$ is holomorphically convex and the projection $p:U\to B$ is its Remmert reduction: $B$ is Stein, $p$ is proper with connected compact normal projective fibres, and the canonical map $\mathcal O_B\to p_*\mathcal O_U$ is an isomorphism. Moreover, $U$ is Stein if and only if $F$ is a point.
  - Explanation: The projection onto the bounded symmetric and affine factors is the Remmert reduction. The cover is Stein exactly when its compact projective factor is a point.
- **claim-singer-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-singer-semialgebraic)): Let $X$ be a smooth connected closed aspherical projective complex $n$-fold whose ordinary universal cover has a semialgebraic projective-open presentation. Its $L^2$-Betti numbers, computed from the reduced $L^2$-cohomology of the universal cover, satisfy $$b_j^{(2)}(X)=0\qquad(j\geq0,\ j\ne n).$$ If $m>0$ for the affine factor in the product constructed in the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, then $b_j^{(2)}(X)=0$ for every $j\geq0$.
  - Explanation: Smooth closed aspherical projective varieties with the specified cover presentation satisfy reduced square-integrable cohomology vanishing away from middle degree. A positive affine factor forces vanishing in every degree.
- **claim-quasiprojective-cover-classification** (classification, [source](#stmt-cor-quasiprojective)): Let $X$ be a connected normal projective variety over $\mathbb C$, with ordinary universal cover $U$. The following are equivalent:* 1. *$U$ is biholomorphic to a quasi-projective variety.* 2. *There are a finite étale Galois cover $X'\to X$, an abelian variety $A$, and a morphism $\alpha:X'\to A$ that is a locally trivial holomorphic fibre bundle with simply connected projective fibre.* 3. *For some integer $m\geq0$ and some simply connected projective variety $F$, there is a biholomorphism $$U\simeq\mathbb C^m\times F.$$* *A point is allowed as the abelian variety or the fibre.* *More generally, let $V\to X$ be a connected infinite étale Galois covering of complex spaces, with $V$ biholomorphic to a quasi-projective variety. There are an intermediate finite étale Galois cover $X'\to X$, a morphism $\alpha:X'\to A$ to an abelian variety that is a locally trivial holomorphic fibre bundle, and an étale cover $\widetilde A\to A$, where $\widetilde A$ has no positive-dimensional compact analytic subvariety, such that $V\to X'$ is biholomorphic as a covering to $$X'\times_A\widetilde A\longrightarrow X'.$$ No algebraicity of the deck transformations is assumed.
  - Explanation: Quasi-projective ordinary universal covers are equivalent to the stated affine-projective products and finite étale Galois bundle covers over abelian varieties. The additional general-cover assertion retains its specified pullback direction.
- **claim-affine-uniformization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-affine-uniformization)): Let $X$ be a connected smooth projective complex $n$-fold, where $n\geq0$. If its ordinary universal cover is biholomorphic to $\mathbb C^n$, then there is a finite étale Galois cover $A\to X$ with $A$ an abelian variety.
  - Explanation: A smooth projective variety whose ordinary universal cover is complex affine space has a finite étale Galois cover by an abelian variety.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prep-generic-distance-convergence",
        "exact_quote": "Let $x$ range over an open real box $U$, let $K$ be compact and globally subanalytic, and let $d_t(x,z)$ be a bounded globally subanalytic family of functions on $K$, for $0<t<t_0$, uniformly Lipschitz in $z$. There is a relatively closed subanalytic subset $E\\subset U$ of dimension smaller than $\\dim U$ such that the extension $$\\widehat d(x,z,t)=\n \\begin{cases}\n d_t(x,z),&t>0,\\\\\n d_0(x,z),&t=0,\n \\end{cases}\n \\qquad d_0(x,z)=\\lim_{t\\downarrow0}d_t(x,z)$$ is jointly continuous at every $(x,z,0)$ with $x\\in U\\setminus E$ and $z\\in K$. In particular, for such $x$, if $x_t\\to x$ and $z_t\\to z$, then $$d_t(x_t,z_t)\\longrightarrow d_0(x,z).$$ For every compact $L\\subset U\\setminus E$, the convergence $d_t\\to d_0$ is uniform on $L\\times K$. One common exceptional set and one smaller open center box may be used for finitely many such tests. For countably many tests one may exclude the union of their exceptional sets, but no common open center box is asserted. These conclusions apply in particular to bounded, capped distance functions of definable closed sets.",
        "source_id": "PM-058.1"
      },

In particular, for such $x$, if $x_t\\to x$ and $z_t\\to z$, then $$d_t(x_t,z_t)\\longrightarrow d_0(x,z).$$ For every compact $L\\subset U\\setminus E$, the convergence $d_t\\to d_0$ is uniform on $L\\times K$. One common exceptional set and one smaller open center box may be used for finitely many such tests. For countably many tests one may exclude the union of their exceptional sets, but no common open center box is asserted. These conclusions apply in particular to bounded, capped distance functions of definable closed sets.",
          "source_id": "PM-058.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prep-generic-freezing",
        "exact_quote": "Let a definable family be written using $\\operatorname{Re}b$ and $\\operatorname{Im}b$, and let $R_t\\to0$ be a fixed real power matrix, independent of the real center $x$. For the substitution $$b=x+R_t(u+iv)$$ its upper-limit and open-kernel tests at generic $x$ are independent of $u$. Thus the kernels are invariant under real translations in the scaled coordinates. The tests may include remaining bounded fibre variables jointly. More generally, when a normalization $R_t(x)$ depends definably on $x$, Lemma <a href=\"#prep:generic-distance-convergence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prep:generic-distance-convergence\">9</a> applies to the normalized closed-set tests themselves.",
        "source_id": "PM-058.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prep-power-diagonalization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $f(e,t,v)$ be a bounded globally subanalytic function with iterated limit $f_{00}(v)=\\lim_{t\\downarrow0}\\lim_{e\\downarrow0}\nf(e,t,v)$. There is an integer $N_0$ such that for every integer $N>N_0$, $$\\lim_{t\\downarrow0}f(t^N,t,v)=f_{00}(v)$$ for each fixed $v$. The same choice works for finitely many tests; the parameter $v$ may include fixed box sizes and positive accuracies. For bounded closed-set tests, apply this to the distance functions on slightly larger boxes, retaining a smaller box to avoid artificial edge effects.",
        "source_id": "PM-058.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-holes-polynomial-maximum",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a complex manifold and let $J\\subset B\\times\\mathbb C$ be closed. Assume that $J\\to B$ is proper, its fibres are locally bounded in the line coordinate, and $(B\\times\\mathbb C)\\setminus J$ is locally pseudoconvex. If $g(b,t)$ is monic of positive degree in $t$ with holomorphic coefficients in $b$, then $$\\psi_g(b)=\\log\\max_{t\\in J_b}|g(b,t)|$$ is plurisubharmonic, with value $-\\infty$ for an empty fibre or a zero maximum. The assertion also holds for polynomials with nowhere-zero holomorphic leading coefficient, and for the constant test $1$.",
        "source_id": "PM-058.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-holes-boundary-trapping",
        "exact_quote": "Let a proper holomorphic submersion be given near a relatively compact parameter interval $I$ on the boundary of a one-sided complex disc. Let $G$ be locally pseudoconvex on that side, and suppose every limit of its fibre holes as the transverse parameter tends to $I$ belongs to a relative divisor $D$ that extends holomorphically across $I$ and is proper in each fibre. Then, after restriction near an interior point of $I$, the holes on that side belong to $D$. The conclusion holds with additional fixed parameters whenever these hypotheses hold locally uniformly in those parameters.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-holes-bounded-containers",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a smooth proper projective family with connected fibres, and a bounded-data semialgebraic family of subsets of those fibres. There is a degree bound, depending only on the family description, such that every member contained in a proper complex algebraic subset is contained in an effective divisor of that degree or smaller. The parameters admitting such a container form a subanalytic set, and containers can be selected subanalytically. For a one-sided family approaching a generic real analytic parameter wall, its limiting containers can be chosen as proper effective divisors varying real analytically on that wall.",
        "source_id": "PM-058.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-holes-projective-propagation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $q:Q\\to B$ be smooth, proper, and projective, with connected smooth fibres. Let $G\\subset Q$ be open, subanalytic, locally pseudoconvex, and fibrewise semialgebraic with bounded data. Put $S=q(G)$, and let $S_0$ be a connected component of $S$. If $G_b$ contains a Zariski open dense subset of $Q_b$ for every $b$ in some nonempty open subset of $S_0$, then $$J=q^{-1}(S_0)\\setminus G$$ is an analytic divisor, possibly empty. Its intersection with each fibre is proper, so every $G_b$, $b\\in S_0$, contains a Zariski open dense subset of $Q_b$.",
        "source_id": "PM-058.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

        "anchor": "stmt-loc-theorem",
        "exact_quote": "Under these hypotheses, at generic $x$, for every finite $L>|p|$ and every $\\eta>0$ there is $t_0=t_0(x,L,\\eta)>0$ such that $$\\label{loc:upper}\n 0<t<t_0\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\n A_t^x(L)\\subset\\{Z:\\operatorname{dist}(Z,\\overline{P_x})<\\eta\\}.$$ Consequently, if $t_n\\downarrow0$ and paths in $D_{t_n}^x$ start at $p$, stay in one fixed bounded box, and have endpoints tending to $z$, then $z\\in\\overline{P_x}$. The same holds for starting points tending to $p$ inside a uniform initial ball.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-loc-puiseux",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U\\subset\\mathbb R^k$ be open and let $f:U\\times(0,\\epsilon)\\to\\mathbb R^m$ be bounded and globally subanalytic. Outside a closed subanalytic subset of $U$ of dimension less than $k$, locally in $x$, there are an integer $q\\geq1$ and a real-analytic map $a(x,s)$, defined near $U'\\times\\{0\\}$, such that $$f(x,t)=a(x,t^{1/q})\\qquad(0<t<\\epsilon').$$ Here $U'$ and $\\epsilon'$ may be reduced. If a scalar coordinate of $f$ is positive, it either has a nonzero limit or, after a further generic restriction, has the form $$t^{\\nu/q}a_0(x,t^{1/q}),\\qquad a_0(x,0)>0,\n                 \\quad \\nu\\in\\mathbb N.$$ On smaller parameter boxes the analytic maps have a common complex neighborhood and all their derivatives are bounded.",
        "source_id": "PM-058.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-loc-paths",
        "exact_quote": "Let $U\\subset\\mathbb R^k$ be open, let $D\\subset U\\times(0,\\epsilon)\\times\\mathbb R^n$ be a bounded globally subanalytic family of open sets, and let $v,w$ be bounded subanalytic sections that belong to the same component of $D_{x,t}$. Locally at generic $x_0\\in U$, there are a neighborhood $U'$ of $x_0$, $\\epsilon'>0$, and paths $$F_t:U'\\times[0,1]\\longrightarrow\\mathbb R^n$$ with endpoints $v(x,t),w(x,t)$ and image in $D_{x,t}$, with the following uniform properties. For each $t$, $F_t(\\cdot,a)$ extends holomorphically to one fixed complex neighborhood $U'_{\\mathbb C}$, independently of $t$ and $a$. On a smaller such neighborhood there is $C<\\infty$ such that $$\\label{loc:path-estimate}\n \\sup_{0<t<\\epsilon'}\\sup_{x\\in U'_{\\mathbb C}}\n \\bigl(|F_t(x,a)|+|F_t(x,a)-F_t(x,b)|/|a-b|\\bigr)\\leq C\n \\quad(a\\ne b).$$ In particular, every fixed order of $x$-derivatives satisfies the same estimate, with its own constant.         "anchor": "stmt-loc-matrix-outer",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose, in addition, that the marked kernel is described recursively by strict positive-definiteness tests for finitely many continuous Hermitian matrix functions $M_j(Z)$, and that every point of $\\overline P$ satisfies $M_j(Z)\\geq0$. Then for each finite $L$, at a generic fixed center, $$\\inf_{Z\\in A_t(L)}\\lambda_{\\min}(M_j(Z))\\geq-o(1)\n                \\qquad(t\\downarrow0)$$ simultaneously for all $j$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-win-plaques",
        "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a regular patch of a real algebraic hypersurface in a smooth complex algebraic $n$-fold. Suppose one side is locally pseudoconvex and the Levi form has constant rank $r$ on $M$. Put $\\ell=n-r-1$. The Levi-null distribution integrates into complex $\\ell$-dimensional plaques. Each sufficiently small plaque is open in an irreducible complex algebraic variety of dimension $\\ell$. Locally at generic transverse parameters these varieties form a real analytic family of bounded degree. After complexifying the parameters, taking the relevant components, and resolving, they have smooth projective models in a smooth proper family.",
        "source_id": "PM-058.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-win-plaques",
          "exact_quote": "Let $M$ be a regular patch of a real algebraic hypersurface in a smooth complex algebraic $n$-fold. Each sufficiently small plaque is open in an irreducible complex algebraic variety of dimension $\\ell$. Locally at generic transverse parameters these varieties form a real analytic family of bounded degree.           "source_id": "PM-058.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-win-period",
        "exact_quote": "In the situation of Lemma <a href=\"#win:plaques\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"win:plaques\">26</a>, there is a real analytic $(r+1)$-parameter family of plaques, with coordinates $(s,w)\\in\n\\mathbb R\\times\\mathbb R^r$, whose complexification evaluates with full complex rank near the original plaque. The remaining $r$ real transverse coordinates can be retained as external parameters. At a fixed real center $x_1$, let $L$ be the smooth projective model of its plaque. There is a symmetric meromorphic $r\\times r$ matrix $H$ on $L$, holomorphic near the original plaque, such that $\\Im H>0$ there and the weighted tangent inequality is $$\\label{win:quadratic-test}\n       q_z(s,w)=\\Im s-(\\Im w)^{\\mathsf T}\n                          (\\Im H(z))^{-1}\\Im w>0.$$ The meromorphic data depend analytically on the generic external parameters.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-win-finite-taylor",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose $A_t(x)$ and $A_t(x)^{-1}$ have joint Puiseux expansions on a real parameter neighborhood, with polynomial bounds in $t^{-1}$, and $R_t(x)$ has the same property and tends to zero locally uniformly. If $f$ is holomorphic on a fixed complex neighborhood, then any expression obtained by multiplying $f(x+R_tz)$ on either side by such Puiseux matrices, and subtracting a similarly bounded constant term, has a polynomial holomorphic limit on every bounded $z$-box whenever it is locally bounded on one nonempty complex open $z$-set. The limit and convergence are analytic in a generic real center and smooth to all orders in $z$ on bounded boxes.*\n\n*In addition, if $R_t$ is invertible and $$C_t(x,u)=R_t(x)^{-1}R_t(x+R_t(x)u)$$ is bounded on real bounded $u$-boxes, it converges smoothly on those boxes to a polynomial $G_x(u)$. After generic restriction, $\\det G_x(u)=1$, and the limiting matrices have polynomial inverses.",
        "source_id": "PM-058.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-win-finite-taylor",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $A_t(x)$ and $A_t(x)^{-1}$ have joint Puiseux expansions on a real parameter neighborhood, with polynomial bounds in $t^{-1}$, and $R_t(x)$ has the same property and tends to zero locally uniformly. If $f$ is holomorphic on a fixed complex neighborhood, then any expression obtained by multiplying $f(x+R_tz)$ on either side by such Puiseux matrices, and subtracting a similarly bounded constant term, has a polynomial holomorphic limit on every bounded $z$-box whenever it is locally bounded on one nonempty complex open $z$-set. The limit and convergence are analytic in a generic real center and smooth to all orders in $z$ on bounded boxes.*\n\n*In addition, if $R_t$ is invertible and $$C_t(x,u)=R_t(x)^{-1}R_t(x+R_t(x)u)$$ is bounded on real bounded $u$-boxes, it converges smoothly on those boxes to a polynomial $G_x(u)$. After generic restriction, $\\det G_x(u)=1$, and the limiting matrices have polynomial inverses.",
          "source_id": "PM-058.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Suppose $A_t(x)$ and $A_t(x)^{-1}$ have joint Puiseux expansions on a real parameter neighborhood, with polynomial bounds in $t^{-1}$, and $R_t(x)$ has the same property and tends to zero locally uniformly.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-win-siegel-normalization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $S_2$ be a fixed parameter domain with normalization $R_t^2(x_2)$, open-kernel component $P_2$, and a fixed interior seed $p_2$. Let $H_*$ be symmetric and holomorphic on a full neighborhood of $x_2$, with $\\Im H_*>0$ on $S_2$. Assume the normalizations have joint Puiseux expansions and polynomial inverse bounds. Define $$\\begin{aligned}\n T_t&=\\bigl(\\Im H_*(x_2+R_t^2p_2)\\bigr)^{1/2},\\label{win:T}\\\\\n H_t(z)&=T_t^{-1}\\bigl(H_*(x_2+R_t^2z)\n             -\\Re H_*(x_2+R_t^2p_2)\\bigr)T_t^{-1}.\\label{win:Ht}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Then $H_t$ converges on all bounded complex boxes to a symmetric holomorphic polynomial matrix $\\tau$, with $\\tau(p_2)=iI$ and $\\Im\\tau>0$ on $P_2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-058.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-win-siegel-normalization",

          "source_id": "PM-058.1"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-win-construction",
        "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a connected smooth projective variety and let $V\\subset Y$ be a nonempty connected semialgebraic open subset that is locally pseudoconvex at its boundary. The following data exist, locally at generic centers in totally real parameter boxes. The same assertion holds in smooth projective families with additional real parameters, analytic coefficients, and bounded algebraic fibre data, when the hypotheses hold fibrewise. The constructed data depend analytically on those parameters after generic restriction and may be complexified locally.*\n\n\n*There is a complex box $B\\subset\\mathbb C^d$, a smooth proper projective family $q:Q\\to B$ with connected fibres of dimension $\\dim Y-d$, and a holomorphic evaluation $\\mathrm{ev}:Q\\to Y$, algebraic with bounded data in the projective variables and of full rank on a coordinate patch meeting every fibre. Its fibre images $Z_b=\\mathrm{ev}(Q_b)$ are irreducible projective varieties of dimension $\\dim Y-d$, and $Q_b\\to Z_b$ is proper and surjective. If $G=\\mathrm{ev}^{-1}(V)$, a component $S$ of $q(G)$ and a relative proper algebraic subset $D\\subset Q$ satisfy $$\\label{win:container}\n               Q_b\\setminus D_b\\subset G_b\\qquad(b\\in S).$$ The container $D$ is analytic on the full box and proper in every fibre, after shrinking the box. The actual holes $Q|_S\\setminus G$ are analytic; their images give precisely $Z_b\\setminus V$ and are algebraic in each fibre. The domain $S$ is locally pseudoconvex at its parameter boundary. Its boundary inside $B$ is disjoint from $q(G)$. All stated shrinkings of $B$ may be made before the choice of scales and sequences.*\n\n\n*There are a fixed interior seed $p$, real block diagonal matrices $R_t(x)$ tending to zero with polynomial inverse bounds, and a component $P$ of the open kernel of $$S_{t,x}=R_t(x)^{-1}(S-x)$$ at $p$. A fixed neighborhood of $p$ is eventually in $S_{t,x}$. The domain $P$ is a finite recursive tower, beginning at a point and adding a block $(s,w)\\in\\mathbb C\\times\\mathbb C^r$ over a previously constructed domain $P_2$ by the condition $$\\label{win:tower}\n      z\\in P_2,\\qquad\n      \\Im M(s,w,z)>0,\\qquad\n      M(s,w,z)=\\begin{pmatrix}s&w^{\\mathsf T}\\\\w&\\tau(z)\\end{pmatrix},$$ where $\\tau$ is symmetric and polynomial holomorphic and $\\Im\\tau>0$ on $P_2$.*\n\n\n\n*For each bounded real $u$-box the ratios $$\\label{win:ratio}\n           R_t(x)^{-1}R_t(x+R_t(x)u)\\longrightarrow G_x(u)$$ converge with every derivative. The matrix $G_x$ is polynomial and block diagonal, each block depends only on later block coordinates, $G_x(0)=I$, and every block has determinant one. Its inverse is polynomial. For all sufficiently small real $u$, $$\\label{win:orbit}\n                           Z\\longmapsto u+G_x(u)Z$$ is an automorphism of $P$.*\n\n*Finally the bounded-path localization conclusion holds: any limit of endpoints of paths in $S_{t,x}$ starting at the seed and staying in a fixed bounded box belongs to $\\overline P$. Equivalently, on each fixed bounded box the accessible components have outer distance to $\\overline P$ tending to zero.",
        "source_id": "PM-058.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-win-construction",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a connected smooth projective variety and let $V\\subset Y$ be a nonempty connected semialgebraic open subset that is locally pseudoconvex at its boundary. The following data exist, locally at generic centers in totally real parameter boxes. The same assertion holds in smooth projective families with additional real parameters, analytic coefficients, and bounded algebraic fibre data, when the hypotheses hold fibrewise. The constructed data depend analytically on those parameters after generic restriction and may be complexified locally.*\n\n\n*There is a complex box $B\\subset\\mathbb C^d$, a smooth proper projective family $q:Q\\to B$ with connected fibres of dimension $\\dim Y-d$, and a holomorphic evaluation $\\mathrm{ev}:Q\\to Y$, algebraic with bounded data in the projective variables and of full rank on a coordinate patch meeting every fibre. Its fibre images $Z_b=\\mathrm{ev}(Q_b)$ are irreducible projective varieties of dimension $\\dim Y-d$, and $Q_b\\to Z_b$ is proper and surjective. If $G=\\mathrm{ev}^{-1}(V)$, a component $S$ of $q(G)$ and a relative proper algebraic subset $D\\subset Q$ satisfy $$\\label{win:container}\n               Q_b\\setminus D_b\\subset G_b\\qquad(b\\in S).$$ The container $D$ is analytic on the full box and proper in every fibre, after shrinking the box. The actual holes $Q|_S\\setminus G$ are analytic; their images give precisely $Z_b\\setminus V$ and are algebraic in each fibre. The domain $S$ is locally pseudoconvex at its parameter boundary. Its boundary inside $B$ is disjoint from $q(G)$. All stated shrinkings of $B$ may be made before the choice of scales and sequences.*\n\n\n*There are a fixed interior seed $p$, real block diagonal matrices $R_t(x)$ tending to zero with polynomial inverse bounds, and a component $P$ of the open kernel of $$S_{t,x}=R_t(x)^{-1}(S-x)$$ at $p$. A fixed neighborhood of $p$ is eventually in $S_{t,x}$. The matrix $G_x$ is polynomial and block diagonal, each block depends only on later block coordinates, $G_x(0)=I$, and every block has determinant one. Its inverse is polynomial. For all sufficiently small real $u$, $$\\label{win:orbit}\n                           Z\\longmapsto u+G_x(u)Z$$ is an automorphism of $P$.*\n\n*Finally the bounded-path localization conclusion holds: any limit of endpoints of paths in $S_{t,x}$ starting at the seed and staying in a fixed bounded box belongs to $\\overline P$.         "exact_quote": "The domain $P$ of Theorem <a href=\"#win:construction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"win:construction\">32</a> is diffeomorphic to $\\mathbb R^{2d}$, where $d=\\dim_{\\mathbb C}P$.         "anchor": "stmt-quo-guard",
        "exact_quote": "There are normalized accessible parameter regions $A_i\\subset\\{|Z|<R_i\\}$, where $R_i\\to\\infty$, and holomorphic functions $h$ on their neighbourhoods, given by the same formula $$\\label{quo:guard-formula}\n h(Z)=\\exp\\left(-\\alpha\\sum_j\\operatorname{tr}\n                      \\log(I-iM_j(Z))\\right),$$ with the following properties. Their values lie in a fixed sector $\\{\\zeta:|\\arg\\zeta|<\\theta\\}$, $\\theta<\\pi/2$, and are uniformly bounded. On $A_i$, $|h(Z)|\\leq C(1+|Z|)^{-\\alpha}$. Every compact subset of $P$ is eventually contained in $A_i$. There are connected open subsets $H_i\\subset A_i$, containing the seed and eventually every compact subset of $P$, such that $$u_i=\\log|h|+a_i|Z|^2>m_i\\quad\\hbox{on }H_i,\n \\qquad a_i>0,$$ where $a_iR_i^2\\leq1$, $m_i\\to-\\infty$, and every boundary of $H_i$ admitting an incidence with a point of $V$ has $|h|\\leq\\delta_i$, with $\\delta_i\\to0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-058.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-quo-guard",
          "exact_quote": "There are normalized accessible parameter regions $A_i\\subset\\{|Z|<R_i\\}$, where $R_i\\to\\infty$, and holomorphic functions $h$ on their neighbourhoods, given by the same formula $$\\label{quo:guard-formula}\n h(Z)=\\exp\\left(-\\alpha\\sum_j\\operatorname{tr}\n                      \\log(I-iM_j(Z))\\right),$$ with the following properties.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-quo-incidence",
        "exact_quote": "The union $U_i=\\bigcup_{b\\in H_i}(Z_b\\cap V)$ is open in $V$. Distinct fibres in this union are disjoint, and there is a holomorphic submersion $$\\pi_i:U_i\\longrightarrow H_i,\n \\qquad \\pi_i^{-1}(b)=Z_b\\cap V.$$ These fibres are connected. Moreover, if $T$ is a smooth connected projective variety, $D\\subset T$ is a proper analytic subset, and $g:T\\setminus D\\to V$ is holomorphic, then any such image meeting a fibre of $\\pi_i$ is contained in that fibre.",
        "source_id": "PM-058.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-quo-cutoff-normal",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be a complex manifold, let $A_i\\subset D$ be open, and let $h_i\\in\\mathcal O(A_i)$ satisfy $|h_i|\\leq C$. Suppose that, for every $x\\in\\partial A_i\\cap D$, $$\\limsup_{A_i\\ni z\\to x}|h_i(z)|\\leq\\epsilon_i,\n \\qquad \\epsilon_i\\longrightarrow0.$$ Extend $h_i$ by zero outside $A_i$. These extended functions have a subsequence converging uniformly on compact subsets of $D$ to a holomorphic function. Suppose in addition that their nonzero values lie in $|\\arg\\zeta|\\leq\\theta<\\pi/2$, that $D$ is connected, and that $x_i\\to x$, $h_i(x_i)\\to a\\ne0$. Then every resulting limit is nowhere zero, and every compact subset of $D$ lies in $A_i$ eventually.",
        "source_id": "PM-058.1"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-quo-trapping",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D_i$ exhaust a connected complex manifold $D$, and let $g_i:D_i\\to V$ be holomorphic.         "anchor": "stmt-quo-projection",
        "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of condition <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a> and the boundary construction of Theorem <a href=\"#win:construction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"win:construction\">32</a>, there are a contractible Stein manifold $M$, biholomorphic to the boundedly realizable tower $P$, an equivariant surjective submersion $f:V\\to M$, and an equivariant holomorphic map $f_X:\\widetilde X\\to M$ with $f=f_X\\mu$. Both maps have connected fibres. Their fibres have simultaneous projective compactifications with analytic holes locally on $M$, after a deck translation. The normal-source fibres are normal and semialgebraically compactifiable up to biholomorphism, and $f_X$ is flat.*\n\n*Suppose additionally that the image $\\Lambda$ of the deck action on $M$ is discrete and acts freely. Then the descended proper map $X\\to M/\\Lambda$, its pullback to $M$, and every connected finite covering family of that pullback are flat and topologically locally trivial, with connected normal projective fibres. Their induced resolution families are smooth proper projective families; the resolution maps are proper, have connected point fibres, and are bimeromorphic on every fibre. The induced maps on integral degree-one cohomology are morphisms of local systems.",

        "anchor": "stmt-cmp-characteristic",
        "exact_quote": "There is a characteristic finite-index subgroup $K'\\subset K$ which is free abelian.       },

        "anchor": "stmt-cor-remmert-semialgebraic",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a connected normal projective complex variety whose ordinary universal cover $U$ has a semialgebraic projective-open presentation. Write $U\\simeq B\\times F$, where $B=D\\times\\mathbb C^m$, as in Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>. Then $U$ is holomorphically convex and the projection $p:U\\to B$ is its Remmert reduction: $B$ is Stein, $p$ is proper with connected compact normal projective fibres, and the canonical map $\\mathcal O_B\\to p_*\\mathcal O_U$ is an isomorphism.         "anchor": "stmt-cor-singer-semialgebraic",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a smooth connected closed aspherical projective complex $n$-fold whose ordinary universal cover has a semialgebraic projective-open presentation. Its $L^2$-Betti numbers, computed from the reduced $L^2$-cohomology of the universal cover, satisfy $$b_j^{(2)}(X)=0\\qquad(j\\geq0,\\ j\\ne n).$$ If $m>0$ for the affine factor in the product constructed in the proof of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, then $b_j^{(2)}(X)=0$ for every $j\\geq0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-058.1"
      },

        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-quasiprojective",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a connected normal projective variety over $\\mathbb C$, with ordinary universal cover $U$. The following are equivalent:*\n\n1.  *$U$ is biholomorphic to a quasi-projective variety.*\n\n2.  *There are a finite étale Galois cover $X'\\to X$, an abelian variety $A$, and a morphism $\\alpha:X'\\to A$ that is a locally trivial holomorphic fibre bundle with simply connected projective fibre.*\n\n3.  *For some integer $m\\geq0$ and some simply connected projective variety $F$, there is a biholomorphism $$U\\simeq\\mathbb C^m\\times F.$$*\n\n*A point is allowed as the abelian variety or the fibre.*\n\n*More generally, let $V\\to X$ be a connected infinite étale Galois covering of complex spaces, with $V$ biholomorphic to a quasi-projective variety. There are an intermediate finite étale Galois cover $X'\\to X$, a morphism $\\alpha:X'\\to A$ to an abelian variety that is a locally trivial holomorphic fibre bundle, and an étale cover $\\widetilde A\\to A$, where $\\widetilde A$ has no positive-dimensional compact analytic subvariety, such that $V\\to X'$ is biholomorphic as a covering to $$X'\\times_A\\widetilde A\\longrightarrow X'.$$ No algebraicity of the deck transformations is assumed.",
        "source_id": "PM-058.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-quasiprojective",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a connected normal projective variety over $\\mathbb C$, with ordinary universal cover $U$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **semialgebraic subset** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A subset of a complex algebraic variety is *semialgebraic* if, in algebraic affine charts, it is defined by finite Boolean combinations of real polynomial equalities and inequalities in the real and imaginary coordinates.
- **semialgebraic projective-open presentation** ([source](#sec:introduction)): We say that a complex space has a *semialgebraic projective-open presentation* if it is biholomorphic to a semialgebraic open subset of a projective variety, with openness taken in the ordinary complex topology.
- **bounded symmetric domain** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A *bounded symmetric domain* is a connected bounded domain in a complex vector space for which every point is an isolated fixed point of a holomorphic involution of the domain.
- **generic real parameters** ([source](#stmt-prep-generic)): An assertion at generic parameters in an open real box means that it holds outside a locally finite union of subanalytic subsets of smaller dimension. When countably many fixed compact boxes or positive accuracies are tested, a countable union of such exceptional sets is permitted. Each application involving finitely many data is subsequently restricted to a neighborhood of the chosen parameter on which those data have the asserted regularity. No uniform neighborhood for all members of a countable list is implicit.
- **local pseudoconvexity** ([source](#pm-h-9)): Local pseudoconvexity means local Steinness at the boundary in the indicated ambient complex manifold.
- **bounded algebraic data** ([source](#prep:section)): In this paper, *bounded algebraic data* means that one such finite atlas and uniform finite bounds for the number and degrees of the polynomial fibre equations and tests have been fixed.
- **bounded-data semialgebraic family** ([source](#prep:section)): A *bounded-data semialgebraic family* has a fixed finite Boolean description of this kind in real and imaginary fibre coordinates.
- **open kernel** ([source](#stmt-prep-kernel)): Let $U_t$ be open subsets of a fixed manifold $M$, $0<t<t_0$. Their open kernel is $$\mathcal K(U_t)=\{z\in M: \text{some neighborhood }W\ni z \text{ satisfies }W\subset U_t \text{ for every sufficiently small }t\}.$$
- **marked kernel** ([source](#stmt-prep-kernel)): A marked kernel is the connected component containing a specified point of the open kernel.
- **accessible component** ([source](#loc:section)): For $L>\|p\|$, let $A_t(L)$ be the component containing $p$ in $D_t\cap B(0,L)$, whenever $p\in D_t$.
- **normalized domains and recentering ratios** ([source](#loc:normalized)): Define $$\label{loc:normalized} D_t^x=\{Z:x+R_t(x)Z\in S\},\qquad C_t(x,u)=R_t(x)^{-1}R_t(x+R_t(x)u).$$
- **joint Puiseux expansion** ([source](#pm-h-22)): In the phrase “joint Puiseux expansion,” a single substitution $t=v^q$ is understood, after which a fixed power of $v$ times each function is analytic on a neighborhood of $\{0\}\times U$.
- **special compact Kähler manifold** ([source](#pm-h-39)): A smooth compact Kähler manifold $Z$ is *special* if it has no Bogomolov sheaf: there is no line bundle $L$ with a nonzero morphism $L\to\Omega_Z^r$ and $\kappa(Z,L)=r>0$.
- **transverse image and fibre kernel** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Put $\Gamma=\pi_1(X)$, let $\Lambda$ be its image in $\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(M)$, and put $K=\ker(\Gamma\to\Lambda)$.
