| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-positive-subanalytic-lower-power | f(v,t) | > | $t^N$ | The exponent can be shared by finitely many functions; the small-scale threshold may depend on the fixed parameter. |
| q-positive-subanalytic-upper-power | f(v,t) | < | $t^{-N}$ | The power is common for a finite collection, while the threshold retains fixed-parameter dependence. |
| q-thin-hole-real-dimension | \dim_{\mathbb R} A | ≤ | $2n-2$ | The real dimension bound is a hypothesis of the analyticity theorem. |
| q-null-plaque-dimension | \ell | = | $n-r-1$ | The ambient complex dimension and Levi rank determine the complex plaque dimension. |
| q-contact-quotient-dimension | real dimension of the contact quotient | = | $2r+1$ | The dimension is determined by the fixed Levi rank. |
| q-recentering-determinant | \det G_x(u) | = | $1$ | The identity holds after generic restriction, with polynomial inverses. |
| q-siegel-seed-value | \tau(p_2) | = | $iI$ | The value is imposed at the fixed lower-stage interior seed. |
| q-tower-real-dimension | real dimension of P | = | $2d$ | The parameter is the complex dimension of the tower. |
| q-guard-sector-angle | \theta | < | $\pi/2$ | A fixed sector controls all guard values in the chosen sequence. |
| q-guard-quadratic-budget | a_iR_i^2 | ≤ | $1$ | The positive quadratic coefficient is chosen relative to the corresponding artificial radius. |
| q-guard-trace-exponent | \alpha | < | $1/(2\sum_jn_j)$ | The positive exponent is selected using the sizes of the finitely many matrix blocks. |
| q-cutoff-approximation-error | \|F_i-h_i\| | ≤ | $4\eta_i$ | The error uses the proof's positive cutoff scale and is local on compact test regions. |
| q-fibre-group-rank | rank of K' | = | $2a$ | The free abelian fibre group is identified integrally with the torus fundamental group. |
| q-singer-off-middle-vanishing | b_j^{(2)}(X) | = | $0$ | Nonnegative cohomological degrees other than the complex dimension. |
| q-affine-factor-total-vanishing | b_j^{(2)}(X) | = | $0$ | All nonnegative cohomological degrees when the affine factor is positive-dimensional. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-power-preparation** [claim](#stmt-prep-puiseux) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-puiseux): Apply one-variable preparation, then restrict to generic analytic coefficient cells and clear fractional-power denominators. -> Common power bounds and joint generic Puiseux expansions.
- **step-projective-models** [claim](#stmt-prep-projective-models) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-projective-models): Track the marked component in projective parameter spaces, resolve evaluation graphs and restrict by generic smoothness. -> Smooth proper connected projective models with holomorphic evaluation.
- **step-kernel-pseudoconvexity** [claim](#stmt-prep-kernel-pseudoconvexity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-kernel-pseudoconvexity): Enlarge compact Hartogs figures inside their kernel neighborhoods and apply local pseudoconvexity before passing to the kernel. -> Local pseudoconvexity of the open kernel.
- **step-finite-jet-specialization** [claim](#stmt-prep-finite-jet-specialization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-finite-jet-specialization): Introduce strict slack and residual tolerances, correct within fixed parameter fibres, truncate analytic coefficients and eliminate real quantifiers. -> Semialgebraic upper limits and complementary open kernels.
- **step-generic-distance-limits** [claim](#stmt-prep-generic-distance-convergence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-generic-distance-convergence): Combine definable pointwise limits, finite nets and generic threshold continuity; remove the lower-dimensional failure-of-continuity locus. -> Joint boundary continuity and compact uniform convergence at generic centers.
- **step-freeze-real-center** [claim](#stmt-prep-generic-freezing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-generic-freezing): Treat the real part as an independent center and apply moving-center continuity to normalized closed-set distance tests. -> Real translation invariance of generic limiting tests.
- **step-power-diagonal** [claim](#stmt-prep-power-diagonalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prep-power-diagonalization): Prepare the positive accuracy threshold in the first scale and choose a higher power diagonal. -> A common diagonal exponent preserving finitely many iterated limits.
- **step-polynomial-hole-tests** [claim](#stmt-holes-polynomial-maximum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-holes-polynomial-maximum): Push holes forward by the polynomial map, preserve pseudoconvexity through its finite fibres and apply the centered Hartogs radius criterion after inversion. -> Plurisubharmonic polynomial maxima, including the constant test.
- **step-boundary-trapping** [claim](#stmt-holes-boundary-trapping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-holes-boundary-trapping): Use Weierstrass cylinders and harmonic-measure comparison to force the polynomial maximum to vanish on one-sided holes. -> Actual nearby one-sided hole containment.
- **step-interior-trapping** [claim](#stmt-holes-interior-trapping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-holes-interior-trapping): Apply uniqueness for a plurisubharmonic function equal to the negative infinite value on a nonpluripolar slice. -> Nearby interior hole containment.
- **step-tangency-propagation** [claim](#stmt-holes-tangency-propagation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-holes-tangency-propagation): Propagate central membership along compact paths using internally tangent discs and the strong maximum principle for their directional radius. -> The entire central divisor complement belongs to the open fibre.
- **step-thin-hole-analyticity** [claim](#stmt-holes-thin-analyticity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-holes-thin-analyticity): Uniformize the thin subanalytic set to obtain locally finite Hausdorff measure, then apply the cited analyticity theorem; handle the curve case directly. -> An empty omission or a pure analytic divisor.
- **step-relative-container** [claim](#stmt-holes-relative-divisor-container) / [proof](#proof-stmt-holes-relative-divisor-container): Restrict by generic flatness, twist the ideal to obtain generated sections and extend a section that remains nonzero on each nearby fibre. -> A proper effective divisor over the full smaller parameter box.
- **step-bounded-container-selection** [claim](#stmt-holes-bounded-containers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-holes-bounded-containers): Bound algebraic closures through elimination, select container cycles and prepare their one-sided wall limits in projective parameter spaces. -> Uniformly bounded container degrees and generic real analytic wall containers.
- **step-projective-hole-propagation** [claim](#stmt-holes-projective-propagation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-holes-projective-propagation): Rule out good-to-bad hypersurface walls using continued containers and tangency; apply thin-set analyticity to the remaining omission. -> Analytic fibrewise proper holes throughout the connected projection component.
- **step-fixed-container-continuation** [claim](#stmt-holes-container-propagation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-holes-container-propagation): Show each hole component dominates the base and continue the container equations by analytic uniqueness. -> Global containment on the selected connected projection component.
- **step-section-test** [claim](#stmt-holes-section-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-holes-section-test): Pull back local pseudoconvexity along a holomorphic section avoiding the container and identify the appropriate projection components. -> Membership detection and local pseudoconvexity of the projection domain.
- **step-analytic-power-path-data** [claim](#stmt-loc-puiseux) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-puiseux): Apply joint preparation to all coordinates, remove negative powers by boundedness and retain nonzero leading coefficients generically. -> Analytic root parametrizations with common complex neighborhoods.
- **step-controlled-paths** [claim](#stmt-loc-paths) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-paths): Use definable triviality, resolve the path graph, join lifted endpoints in monomial charts and reparametrize by a center-independent clock. -> Replacement paths with uniform holomorphic center dependence and path regularity.
- **step-accessible-localization** [claim](#stmt-loc-theorem) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-paths-2): Solve triangular attached-disc equations with full center rank, persist them at finite scale and approach controlled path endpoints; exclude generic failure loci. -> Fixed-box accessible upper containment in the marked kernel closure.
- **step-semidefinite-outer-tests** [claim](#stmt-loc-matrix-outer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-loc-matrix-outer): Contradict a negative eigenvalue gap by compact subsequence convergence and continuity at the localized endpoint. -> Simultaneous asymptotic semidefinite outer tests on each fixed box.
- **step-algebraic-plaques** [claim](#stmt-win-plaques) / [proof](#proof-stmt-win-plaques): Integrate the Levi-null distribution, polarize the algebraic defining equation and track the resulting isotropic algebraic components. -> Bounded-degree algebraic null plaques with smooth projective family models.
- **step-period-matrix** [claim](#stmt-win-period) / [proof](#proof-stmt-win-period): Choose a contact Lagrangian family, compare infinitesimal symplectic frames and compute the weighted boundary Taylor polynomial. -> Symmetric meromorphic period matrix and the positive quadratic tangent inequality.
- **step-whole-seed-fibre** [claim](#stmt-win-seed-bergman) / [proof](#proof-stmt-win-seed-bergman): Identify the entire fibre by compact-box Taylor convergence and map its positive quadratic tube explicitly to the ball. -> Whole marked seed fibre and a nonvanishing Bergman evaluation.
- **step-exact-epigraph** [claim](#stmt-win-first-epigraph) / [proof](#proof-stmt-win-first-epigraph): Classify generic tube fibres, exclude affine lines by Bergman variation, extend the period matrix by Cayley contraction and eliminate remaining analytic omissions. -> An exact positive quadratic epigraph over the lower projective domain.
- **step-finite-taylor** [claim](#stmt-win-finite-taylor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-win-finite-taylor): Expand through a sufficiently high finite Taylor order and force every negative Laurent coefficient to vanish by boundedness on an open set. -> Polynomial holomorphic limits and unimodular polynomial recentering limits.
- **step-normalize-siegel** [claim](#stmt-win-siegel-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-win-siegel-normalization): Normalize by the seed imaginary square root, obtain local bounds through Cayley transforms and apply finite Taylor extraction and the strong minimum principle. -> Polynomial symmetric limit with strict positive imaginary part on the lower tower.
- **step-projective-window-induction** [claim](#stmt-win-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-win-construction): Induct on projective dimension, compose whole-fibre families, trap holes in continued containers, identify marked iterated kernels and select a power diagonal compatible with triangular ratios. -> Projective windows, recursive polynomial tower, recentering automorphisms and bounded-path localization.
- **step-tower-geometry** [claim](#stmt-win-tower-properties) / [proof](#proof-stmt-win-tower-properties): Use Schur-complement slack coordinates, a logarithmic matrix exhaustion, a bounded first-column realization and triangular infinitesimal automorphisms. -> Euclidean diffeomorphism, Steinness, bounded realization and complex-spanning complete fields.
- **step-cover-pseudoconvexity** [claim](#stmt-quo-pseudoconvex) / [proof](#proof-stmt-quo-pseudoconvex): Extend the covering map across Hartogs figures and use evenly covered target neighborhoods to rule out a first boundary exit. -> Local pseudoconvexity in the smooth projective ambient variety.
- **step-proper-kobayashi-distance** [claim](#stmt-quo-tower) / [proof](#proof-stmt-quo-tower): Embed the tower closedly into a product of Siegel spaces and use their compact distance balls together with bounded realization. -> A proper Kobayashi distance inducing the ordinary tower topology.
- **step-guards** [claim](#stmt-quo-guard) / [proof](#proof-stmt-quo-guard): Choose scales diagonally using fixed-ball semidefinite tests, form trace-logarithmic guards and cut out their strictly plurisubharmonic high regions. -> Exhausting high parameter regions with bounded decaying sector-valued guards.
- **step-incidence-rigidity** [claim](#stmt-quo-incidence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-quo-incidence): Sum bounded plurisubharmonic parameter traces, use constancy on projective-open models and identify the finite reduced incidence graph over the normal target. -> Disjoint intrinsic connected fibres and a holomorphic submersion.
- **step-cutoff-compactness** [claim](#stmt-quo-cutoff-normal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-quo-cutoff-normal): Control cutoff energy and the Cauchy–Riemann defect, upgrade sliced compactness to uniform convergence and use sector positivity to prevent zero limits. -> Holomorphic compact limits and eventual compact containment under the added sector assumptions.
- **step-chart-trapping** [claim](#stmt-quo-trapping) / [proof](#proof-stmt-quo-trapping): Apply cutoff compactness to pulled-back guards, bound normalized parameters by guard decay and combine localization with harmonic positivity. -> Eventual chart containment and normal limits into the strict tower.
- **step-inverse-tightness** [claim](#stmt-quo-inverse-tightness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-quo-inverse-tightness): Assume failure of fixed-chart compact containment and test a diagonally chosen inverse subsequence against later chart guards. -> Compact inverse images inside a single fixed quotient chart.
- **step-normal-descent** [claim](#stmt-quo-descent) / [proof](#proof-stmt-quo-descent): Use bounded coordinates to make the quotient constant on modification fibres, descend by normal removability and push forward compact incidence families. -> Unique equivariant normal-source map with connected semialgebraically compactifiable fibres.
- **step-intrinsic-projection** [claim](#stmt-quo-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-quo-projection): Prepare normal flat compactifying families, fibrewise resolutions and intrinsic stratum submersions before scaling; propagate by exhaustion and apply proper isotopy after a free discrete quotient. -> The intrinsic projection with full normal-source regularity and conditional proper-family cohomology interfaces.
- **step-abelian-liouville** [claim](#stmt-grp-liouville) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grp-liouville): Pass to an abelian finite-index deck group, analyze minimal positive harmonic rays by Harnack translation and integrate their descended logarithmic differential. -> Constancy of bounded holomorphic functions.
- **step-proper-level-projection** [claim](#stmt-grp-finite-level-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grp-finite-level-projection): Resolve the meromorphic graph, force each level component meeting the valid open locus to have constant parameter and use proper compactness to count those components locally. -> Local finiteness of the attained level parameters.
- **step-core-levels** [claim](#stmt-grp-countable-core-level) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grp-countable-core-level): Apply compact special abelianity and Liouville to lifted core fibres, extend the core over full projective incidence families and identify attained levels using whole-leaf surjectivity. -> Countable locally finite invariant core-level images with dense union.
- **step-transverse-discreteness** [claim](#stmt-grp-discrete) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grp-discrete): Show the identity component of the closure fixes every locally finite invariant level and hence fixes the whole base; use the compact source quotient for cocompactness. -> Discrete cocompact transverse image.
- **step-free-finite-index** [claim](#stmt-grp-free-finite-index) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grp-free-finite-index): Apply Selberg's lemma to the adjoint image, eliminate finite stabilizers using the complex-spanning fields and algebraize the corresponding finite cover. -> Free torsion-free finite-index action and a normal projective finite cover.
- **step-symmetric-base** [claim](#stmt-grp-discrete-symmetric) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grp-discrete-symmetric): Descend Bergman positivity to the compact quotient, apply the aspherical Nadel–Frankel splitting and eliminate its discrete-automorphism factor using complete fields and de Rham uniqueness. -> A bounded symmetric intrinsic base.
- **step-compactifiable-cover-group** [claim](#stmt-grp-compactifiable-cover) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grp-compactifiable-cover): Extend the orbifold core across the analytic boundary, count ordinary lifted level components and identify their finite-index monodromy with a compact special fibre image. -> Virtually abelian deck group.
- **step-fibre-kernel** [claim](#stmt-grp-kernel-abelian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grp-kernel-abelian): Identify each leaf as a connected regular cover of a smooth compact fibre and use compactifiability and surjective fibre monodromy. -> Finite generation and virtual abelianity of the transverse kernel.
- **step-characteristic-cover** [claim](#stmt-cmp-characteristic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-characteristic): Intersect the finitely many subgroups below a fixed index bound and use the contractible-base bundle interface to identify fibre fundamental groups. -> Characteristic free abelian fibre cover and connected universal fibre covers.
- **step-albanese-descent** [claim](#stmt-cmp-albanese) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-albanese): Embed normal-fibre integral cohomology into the resolution variation, construct the weight-one torus quotient and descend its relative Albanese map along connected modification fibres. -> Equivariant Albanese torsors and fundamental-group isomorphisms.
- **step-affine-base** [claim](#stmt-cmp-affine-base) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-affine-base): Apply the entire-pullback bundle theorem fibrewise, prove total flatness, lift to the Albanese universal cover and trivialize the resulting affine bundle over the contractible Stein base. -> Proper flat map to the symmetric-affine product with simply connected normal projective fibres.
- **step-invariant-rigidity** [claim](#stmt-cmp-closed-rigidity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-closed-rigidity): Build a controlled proper map into the invariant subset from translated finite-type homotopies and compare its proper degree with the identity. -> Equality of the invariant subset with the whole base.
- **step-chow-base** [claim](#stmt-cmp-chow-base) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-chow-base): Use the proper flat cycle family, integral-cycle conditions and real quantifier elimination; identify every supported integral cycle as an entire fibre by Stein separation. -> Semialgebraic Chow model homeomorphic to the affine base.
- **step-polarized-loci** [claim](#stmt-cmp-polarized-locus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-polarized-locus): Bound graph degree by constant integral intersections, describe graph isomorphisms semialgebraically and retain the components with the correct integral polarization marking. -> Semialgebraic polarized isomorphism classes.
- **step-compact-product** [claim](#stmt-cmp-product) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-product): Force invariant class closures to fill the base, deduce a single semialgebraic polarized class, form local Hilbert products and trivialize the principal automorphism bundle by the Oka principle. -> Global product with a simply connected normal projective compact factor.
- **step-converse-presentation** [claim](#stmt-cmp-converse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cmp-converse): Realize the bounded symmetric domain as a semialgebraic open orbit in its compact dual and multiply by the affine projective chart and compact factor. -> A semialgebraic projective-open presentation.
- **step-classification-assembly** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#sec:assembly): Assemble the intrinsic projection, transverse symmetry, virtually abelian kernel and compact product, and combine with the converse presentation. -> The claimed product classification in the normal-projective scope.
- **step-remmert-reduction** [claim](#stmt-cor-remmert-semialgebraic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-remmert-semialgebraic): Use constancy of holomorphic functions on the compact connected normal factor to identify the direct-image sheaf and holomorphic hulls. -> Holomorphic convexity, the Remmert reduction and the compact-factor Stein criterion.
- **step-singer-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-cor-singer-semialgebraic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-singer-semialgebraic): Remove the compact factor by asphericity; use the infinite normal amenable subgroup when the affine factor is present and symmetric-space harmonic vanishing otherwise. -> Off-middle reduced square-integrable cohomology vanishing and total vanishing with a positive affine factor.
- **step-quasiprojective-refinement** [claim](#stmt-cor-quasiprojective) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-quasiprojective): Apply the zero-boundary log-abundance theorem to supply smooth abundance, then invoke the cited quasi-projective-cover classification and general-cover theorem. -> Finite étale Galois abelian-base bundle refinement and the stated general-cover pullback.
- **step-affine-uniformization** [claim](#stmt-cor-affine-uniformization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-affine-uniformization): Lift the compact bundle fibre into affine space and use constancy of its coordinate functions to make that fibre a point. -> A finite étale Galois cover by an abelian variety.

The parameters admitting such a container form a subanalytic set, and containers can be selected subanalytically.           "anchor": "stmt-loc-puiseux",
          "exact_quote": "Let $U\\subset\\mathbb R^k$ be open and let $f:U\\times(0,\\epsilon)\\to\\mathbb R^m$ be bounded and globally subanalytic. If a scalar coordinate of $f$ is positive, it either has a nonzero limit or, after a further generic restriction, has the form $$t^{\\nu/q}a_0(x,t^{1/q}),\\qquad a_0(x,0)>0,\n                 \\quad \\nu\\in\\mathbb N.$$ On smaller parameter boxes the analytic maps have a common complex neighborhood and all their derivatives are bounded.",

        }

          "exact_quote": "Let $U\\subset\\mathbb R^k$ be open, let $D\\subset U\\times(0,\\epsilon)\\times\\mathbb R^n$ be a bounded globally subanalytic family of open sets, and let $v,w$ be bounded subanalytic sections that belong to the same component of $D_{x,t}$. Locally at generic $x_0\\in U$, there are a neighborhood $U'$ of $x_0$, $\\epsilon'>0$, and paths $$F_t:U'\\times[0,1]\\longrightarrow\\mathbb R^n$$ with endpoints $v(x,t),w(x,t)$ and image in $D_{x,t}$, with the following uniform properties. For each $t$, $F_t(\\cdot,a)$ extends holomorphically to one fixed complex neighborhood $U'_{\\mathbb C}$, independently of $t$ and $a$. On a smaller such neighborhood there is $C<\\infty$ such that $$\\label{loc:path-estimate}\n \\sup_{0<t<\\epsilon'}\\sup_{x\\in U'_{\\mathbb C}}\n \\bigl(|F_t(x,a)|+|F_t(x,a)-F_t(x,b)|/|a-b|\\bigr)\\leq C\n \\quad(a\\ne b).$$ In particular, every fixed order of $x$-derivatives satisfies the same estimate, with its own constant.           "anchor": "stmt-loc-theorem",

          "anchor": "stmt-win-finite-taylor",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $A_t(x)$ and $A_t(x)^{-1}$ have joint Puiseux expansions on a real parameter neighborhood, with polynomial bounds in $t^{-1}$, and $R_t(x)$ has the same property and tends to zero locally uniformly. If $f$ is holomorphic on a fixed complex neighborhood, then any expression obtained by multiplying $f(x+R_tz)$ on either side by such Puiseux matrices, and subtracting a similarly bounded constant term, has a polynomial holomorphic limit on every bounded $z$-box whenever it is locally bounded on one nonempty complex open $z$-set. The limit and convergence are analytic in a generic real center and smooth to all orders in $z$ on bounded boxes.*\n\n*In addition, if $R_t$ is invertible and $$C_t(x,u)=R_t(x)^{-1}R_t(x+R_t(x)u)$$ is bounded on real bounded $u$-boxes, it converges smoothly on those boxes to a polynomial $G_x(u)$. After generic restriction, $\\det G_x(u)=1$, and the limiting matrices have polynomial inverses.",
          "source_id": "PM-058.1"
        }

Define $$\\begin{aligned}\n T_t&=\\bigl(\\Im H_*(x_2+R_t^2p_2)\\bigr)^{1/2},\\label{win:T}\\\\\n H_t(z)&=T_t^{-1}\\bigl(H_*(x_2+R_t^2z)\n             -\\Re H_*(x_2+R_t^2p_2)\\bigr)T_t^{-1}.\\label{win:Ht}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Then $H_t$ converges on all bounded complex boxes to a symmetric holomorphic polynomial matrix $\\tau$, with $\\tau(p_2)=iI$ and $\\Im\\tau>0$ on $P_2$.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-win-construction",
          "exact_quote": "Let $Y$ be a connected smooth projective variety and let $V\\subset Y$ be a nonempty connected semialgebraic open subset that is locally pseudoconvex at its boundary. The following data exist, locally at generic centers in totally real parameter boxes. The same assertion holds in smooth projective families with additional real parameters, analytic coefficients, and bounded algebraic fibre data, when the hypotheses hold fibrewise. The constructed data depend analytically on those parameters after generic restriction and may be complexified locally.*\n\n\n*There is a complex box $B\\subset\\mathbb C^d$, a smooth proper projective family $q:Q\\to B$ with connected fibres of dimension $\\dim Y-d$, and a holomorphic evaluation $\\mathrm{ev}:Q\\to Y$, algebraic with bounded data in the projective variables and of full rank on a coordinate patch meeting every fibre. Its fibre images $Z_b=\\mathrm{ev}(Q_b)$ are irreducible projective varieties of dimension $\\dim Y-d$, and $Q_b\\to Z_b$ is proper and surjective. If $G=\\mathrm{ev}^{-1}(V)$, a component $S$ of $q(G)$ and a relative proper algebraic subset $D\\subset Q$ satisfy $$\\label{win:container}\n               Q_b\\setminus D_b\\subset G_b\\qquad(b\\in S).$$ The container $D$ is analytic on the full box and proper in every fibre, after shrinking the box. The actual holes $Q|_S\\setminus G$ are analytic; their images give precisely $Z_b\\setminus V$ and are algebraic in each fibre. The domain $S$ is locally pseudoconvex at its parameter boundary. Its boundary inside $B$ is disjoint from $q(G)$. All stated shrinkings of $B$ may be made before the choice of scales and sequences.*\n\n\n*There are a fixed interior seed $p$, real block diagonal matrices $R_t(x)$ tending to zero with polynomial inverse bounds, and a component $P$ of the open kernel of $$S_{t,x}=R_t(x)^{-1}(S-x)$$ at $p$. A fixed neighborhood of $p$ is eventually in $S_{t,x}$. The domain $P$ is a finite recursive tower, beginning at a point and adding a block $(s,w)\\in\\mathbb C\\times\\mathbb C^r$ over a previously constructed domain $P_2$ by the condition $$\\label{win:tower}\n      z\\in P_2,\\qquad\n      \\Im M(s,w,z)>0,\\qquad\n      M(s,w,z)=\\begin{pmatrix}s&w^{\\mathsf T}\\\\w&\\tau(z)\\end{pmatrix},$$ where $\\tau$ is symmetric and polynomial holomorphic and $\\Im\\tau>0$ on $P_2$.*\n\n\n\n*For each bounded real $u$-box the ratios $$\\label{win:ratio}\n           R_t(x)^{-1}R_t(x+R_t(x)u)\\longrightarrow G_x(u)$$ converge with every derivative. The matrix $G_x$ is polynomial and block diagonal, each block depends only on later block coordinates, $G_x(0)=I$, and every block has determinant one. Its inverse is polynomial. For all sufficiently small real $u$, $$\\label{win:orbit}\n                           Z\\longmapsto u+G_x(u)Z$$ is an automorphism of $P$.*\n\n*Finally the bounded-path localization conclusion holds: any limit of endpoints of paths in $S_{t,x}$ starting at the seed and staying in a fixed bounded box belongs to $\\overline P$. Equivalently, on each fixed bounded box the accessible components have outer distance to $\\overline P$ tending to zero.",
          "source_id": "PM-058.1"
        }
      ],

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-quo-guard",
          "exact_quote": "There are normalized accessible parameter regions $A_i\\subset\\{|Z|<R_i\\}$, where $R_i\\to\\infty$, and holomorphic functions $h$ on their neighbourhoods, given by the same formula $$\\label{quo:guard-formula}\n h(Z)=\\exp\\left(-\\alpha\\sum_j\\operatorname{tr}\n                      \\log(I-iM_j(Z))\\right),$$ with the following properties. Their values lie in a fixed sector $\\{\\zeta:|\\arg\\zeta|<\\theta\\}$, $\\theta<\\pi/2$, and are uniformly bounded. On $A_i$, $|h(Z)|\\leq C(1+|Z|)^{-\\alpha}$. Every compact subset of $P$ is eventually contained in $A_i$. There are connected open subsets $H_i\\subset A_i$, containing the seed and eventually every compact subset of $P$, such that $$u_i=\\log|h|+a_i|Z|^2>m_i\\quad\\hbox{on }H_i,\n \\qquad a_i>0,$$ where $a_iR_i^2\\leq1$, $m_i\\to-\\infty$, and every boundary of $H_i$ admitting an incidence with a point of $V$ has $|h|\\leq\\delta_i$, with $\\delta_i\\to0$.",

        }

          "anchor": "stmt-quo-incidence",

Moreover, if $T$ is a smooth connected projective variety, $D\\subset T$ is a proper analytic subset, and $g:T\\setminus D\\to V$ is holomorphic, then any such image meeting a fibre of $\\pi_i$ is contained in that fibre.",

These extended functions have a subsequence converging uniformly on compact subsets of $D$ to a holomorphic function. Suppose in addition that their nonzero values lie in $|\\arg\\zeta|\\leq\\theta<\\pi/2$, that $D$ is connected, and that $x_i\\to x$, $h_i(x_i)\\to a\\ne0$. Then every resulting limit is nowhere zero, and every compact subset of $D$ lies in $A_i$ eventually.",

        }

Both maps have connected fibres. Their fibres have simultaneous projective compactifications with analytic holes locally on $M$, after a deck translation. The normal-source fibres are normal and semialgebraically compactifiable up to biholomorphism, and $f_X$ is flat.*\n\n*Suppose additionally that the image $\\Lambda$ of the deck action on $M$ is discrete and acts freely. Then the descended proper map $X\\to M/\\Lambda$, its pullback to $M$, and every connected finite covering family of that pullback are flat and topologically locally trivial, with connected normal projective fibres. Their induced resolution families are smooth proper projective families; the resolution maps are proper, have connected point fibres, and are bimeromorphic on every fibre. <a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-universal-cover-classification**: For a connected normal projective complex variety, a semialgebraic projective-open presentation of its ordinary universal cover is equivalent to a holomorphic product decomposition into a bounded symmetric domain, a complex affine space, and a simply connected normal projective variety. Point factors are permitted, and the compact factor may be singular.
- **invariant-neighborhood-kernel**: Membership in an open kernel requires eventual inclusion of a neighborhood. A marked kernel selects the connected component containing the specified seed; pointwise eventual membership does not replace the neighborhood condition.
- **invariant-generic-localization**: At generic real centers, endpoints of paths starting at the seed and remaining in a fixed bounded box can accumulate only in the closure of the marked kernel. The threshold depends on the fixed center, radius, and accuracy.
- **invariant-transverse-symmetry**: Under the intrinsic-projection hypotheses, the transverse deck image is discrete and cocompact. After a finite cover it acts freely, and the transverse manifold is biholomorphic to a bounded symmetric domain, allowing a point.
- **invariant-affine-base-and-compact-factor**: The proper flat projection to the affine base has connected, simply connected, normal projective fibres. The resulting family is holomorphically a product with a simply connected normal projective variety.
- **invariant-remmert-reduction**: The projection onto the bounded symmetric and affine factors is the Remmert reduction. The universal cover is holomorphically convex and is Stein precisely when the compact projective factor is a point.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-arbitrary-complex-spaces**: Apply the product classification to arbitrary compact complex spaces or non-normal projective spaces. -> The classification supplies no such extension, and its compact-factor argument retains normal singularities.
- **anti-use-algebraic-deck-action**: Represent the deck action by algebraic transformations merely because the cover has a semialgebraic presentation. -> The chosen semialgebraic structure need not be preserved by deck transformations.
- **anti-use-pointwise-kernel**: Identify the open kernel by testing eventual membership of individual points. -> A point can pass an eventual-membership test without the neighborhood inclusion needed to retain interior geometry.
- **anti-use-moving-fixed-parameters**: Move auxiliary parameters while correcting residual errors using an estimate proved with those parameters fixed. -> The correction can leave the fibre on which the distance estimate was established.
- **anti-use-uncontrolled-joint-limits**: Let the accessible radius grow while the scale shrinks arbitrarily and infer the fixed-box outer estimate. -> The localization assertion controls accessible components only after a finite box and accuracy have been fixed.
- **anti-use-countability-as-discreteness**: Deduce transverse discreteness directly from countability of core-level images. -> Countability alone does not establish the local finiteness required by the closure-of-the-action argument.
- **anti-use-finite-homotopy-type-only**: Infer an Albanese fibre bundle from finite-CW homotopy type of the universal cover alone. -> The source records examples with that homotopy property whose Albanese maps are not differentiable fibre bundles.
- **anti-use-smooth-compact-factor**: Replace the normal compact factor by a smooth resolution while preserving the stated product conclusion. -> A resolution changes the compact complex space, whereas the asserted product preserves the original normal universal cover.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The classification concerns holomorphic existence and global product structure of ordinary universal covers, not a finite constructive input-output problem. Its proof requires analytic limits and a global bundle trivialization. The compact-factor proof states: "Since $T$ is contractible and paracompact, that principal bundle is topologically trivial." Finite matrix calculations within the proof would not certify the classification, the existence of the intrinsic quotient, or the holomorphic trivialization over the Stein base. A finite executable construction and an independent decision oracle for the full result are ABSENT.

The construction concerns the biholomorphism type of the covering space. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-normal-projective-source** (hypothesis): The classification requires a connected normal projective complex variety and its ordinary universal cover. Blocked use: ['Apply the classification to arbitrary compact complex spaces, non-normal projective spaces, or arbitrary covering spaces.']
- **limit-analytic-presentation-and-deck-action** (formal_scope): The semialgebraic presentation concerns the complex space up to biholomorphism and imposes no algebraicity on deck transformations. Blocked use: ['Assume that deck transformations preserve the chosen semialgebraic presentation or extend algebraically to its projective ambient variety.']
- **limit-singular-and-point-factors** (edge_case): The compact factor is normal and projective and may be singular; point factors are permitted. Blocked use: ['Replace the compact factor by a smooth resolution while claiming the same biholomorphic decomposition.', 'Exclude the point cases from the classification.']
- **limit-generic-parameter-neighborhoods** (hypothesis): Generic conclusions exclude the specified smaller-dimensional subanalytic exceptional sets. Countable tests do not provide a common open neighborhood on which all tests have the asserted regularity. Blocked use: ['Apply a generic conclusion at every parameter.', 'Choose a common regularity neighborhood for an entire countable collection solely from genericity.']
- **limit-fixed-parameter-thresholds** (approximation): Common preparation exponents do not make small-scale thresholds uniform in the fixed auxiliary parameters, and corrections must retain the parameters for which the estimate was proved. Blocked use: ['Move auxiliary parameters during a correction justified only by a fixed-parameter distance estimate.', 'Infer uniform thresholds from the existence of a common power exponent.']
- **limit-projective-model-generic-branch** (hypothesis): Tracking a marked irreducible member requires a local branch near generic totally real parameters and the permitted shrinkings of the family. Blocked use: ['Continue the selected smooth projective model across component changes or branch loci without additional justification.']
- **limit-neighborhood-kernel** (formal_scope): Open-kernel membership requires eventual inclusion of a neighborhood, and the marked kernel retains only the component containing the specified seed. Blocked use: ['Replace neighborhood inclusion by pointwise eventual membership.', 'Treat every component of the full limiting kernel as part of the marked component.']
- **limit-finite-jet-regularity** (hypothesis): The semialgebraic specialization conclusion requires polynomial fibre tests, analytic coefficients, the specified Puiseux expansions, and power bounds for the normalization and its inverse. Merely globally subanalytic input gives a subanalytic conclusion. Blocked use: ['Assert semialgebraicity for arbitrary globally subanalytic coefficient data.', 'Discard inverse power bounds when transferring residual corrections to normalized coordinates.']
- **limit-center-freezing-normalization** (hypothesis): The direct real-center freezing assertion uses a real power matrix independent of the center. Center-dependent normalization must be handled through the normalized closed-set tests. Blocked use: ['Freeze a center-dependent normalization by applying the center-independent argument before normalization.']
- **limit-power-diagonal-order** (approximation): The power diagonal preserves the stated iterated limit for fixed auxiliary parameters after choosing a sufficiently large exponent; it does not license an arbitrary relation between the scales. Blocked use: ['Replace the prepared power diagonal by an arbitrary simultaneous approach of the scales.', 'Claim uniform convergence thresholds over moving accuracies or box sizes.']
- **limit-hole-trapping-container** (hypothesis): Hole trapping requires local pseudoconvexity and confinement, together with a divisor extending over the required parameter neighborhood and remaining proper in each fibre. Interior trapping also requires a nonpluripolar slice. Blocked use: ['Propagate a container defined only on a parameter edge without its required continuation.', 'Deduce nearby hole containment from containment on an arbitrary thin parameter slice.']
- **limit-tangency-connected-complement** (hypothesis): Tangency propagation uses a connected central divisor complement, a nonempty central open fibre, and confinement of every relevant one-sided hole limit. Blocked use: ['Propagate membership to disconnected central components from a seed in only one component.', 'Infer central membership when the central open fibre is empty.']
- **limit-thin-hole-analyticity** (hypothesis): The thin-hole theorem requires a closed subanalytic omission with the stated real dimension bound and locally pseudoconvex complement. Its conclusion permits an empty omission. Blocked use: ['Infer analyticity of an arbitrary closed omission from small dimension alone.', 'Conclude that an analytic divisor must be nonempty.']
- **limit-bounded-projective-hole-propagation** (hypothesis): Projective-fibre propagation uses smooth proper projective families with connected smooth fibres, bounded fibrewise semialgebraic data, local pseudoconvexity, and an initial nonempty open region of Zariski open dense fibre portions. Blocked use: ['Propagate Zariski openness from a single isolated good parameter.', 'Transfer the propagation conclusion to nonproper families or families without the stated bounded algebraic data.']
- **limit-accessible-fixed-box-localization** (approximation): Accessible upper containment holds at generic centers for paths starting at the seed and remaining in a fixed bounded box, under the triangular polynomial recentering and invertibility assumptions. Blocked use: ['Allow the accessible radius to grow arbitrarily while the scale decreases.', 'Apply localization to unrelated domains lacking the fixed original domain and the prescribed recentering ratios.', 'Replace asymptotic outer containment by exact finite-scale containment.']
- **limit-replacement-path-regularity** (formal_scope): Replacement paths require same-component endpoint sections in a bounded globally subanalytic open family. Their timing and the replacement paths themselves need not be subanalytic. Blocked use: ['Use the replacement construction for endpoints in different components.', 'Assert definability of the chosen path timing from its uniform analytic and Lipschitz estimates.']
- **limit-joint-puiseux-and-open-set-bounds** (hypothesis): Finite Taylor extraction requires joint Puiseux expansions, polynomial bounds, a fixed holomorphic neighborhood, and local boundedness on a nonempty complex open set. Pointwise expansions or boundedness only at the seed do not supply these hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Infer a polynomial holomorphic limit from separate expansions at individual centers.', 'Replace open-set boundedness by a sampled or seed-only bound.']
- **limit-generic-window-construction** (nonconstructive): The projective-window construction is local at generic centers and depends on analytic preparation, component selection, and scale choices. A finite recognition procedure for arbitrary encoded projective-open presentations is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Expose the existence theorem as an automatic universal-cover decomposition algorithm.', 'Treat bounded algebraic fibre data as an explicit computational complexity guarantee.']
- **limit-guards-and-inverse-compactness** (hypothesis): The quotient argument needs guards with vanishing internal boundary bounds and inverse images of compact sets contained in a compact subset of a fixed chart. Forward normal convergence alone does not establish the inverse biholomorphism. Blocked use: ['Identify the quotient with the tower using only convergence of forward maps.', 'Replace fixed-chart compact containment by containment in a sequence of changing charts.']
- **limit-normal-descent-and-free-quotient** (hypothesis): Normal-source descent retains normality rather than replacing the source by its resolution. The proper projective-family conclusions additionally require the transverse image to be discrete and free. Blocked use: ['Use the conditional proper-family conclusions before establishing discreteness and freeness.', 'Infer normality of every fibre merely from normality of the total space.']
- **limit-full-incidence-and-proper-levels** (hypothesis): Local finiteness of countable attained level images requires the proper family and meromorphic extension. The application to core levels also retains representatives in the full incidence locus, including those in the container. Blocked use: ['Infer local finiteness or transverse discreteness from countability alone.', 'Replace surjectivity onto entire leaves by a parametrization of a dense open subset.']
- **limit-external-special-abelianity** (formal_scope): Virtual abelianity of compact special fundamental groups is an external input applied to smooth compact special manifolds. The subsequent transverse and fibre-kernel conclusions depend on that input. Blocked use: ['Describe compact special abelianity as proved within this classification argument.', 'Apply its interface to arbitrary singular or noncompact special spaces.']
- **limit-free-cover-before-fibre-kernel** (hypothesis): The fibre-kernel theorem is stated after the finite-cover replacement giving a free transverse action. The kernel is virtually abelian, and a characteristic finite-index subgroup is then chosen to obtain a free abelian fibre group. Blocked use: ['Identify the original kernel with a free abelian group without the finite-index construction.', 'Use the regular fibre-cover identification before the stated free-action replacement.']
- **limit-entire-albanese-pullback** (hypothesis): The Albanese bundle argument retains the entire semialgebraic pullback. Finite CW homotopy type or a semialgebraic dense open part does not replace that requirement. Blocked use: ['Deduce the Albanese bundle conclusion from finite CW homotopy type alone.', 'Apply the bundle theorem after discarding part of the pullback.']
- **limit-polarization-and-global-trivialization** (nonconstructive): The compact-factor product uses semialgebraic polarized isomorphism classes, invariant closed-subset rigidity, local holomorphic products, and the Oka principle over a contractible Stein base. It does not provide explicit global trivializing coordinates. Blocked use: ['Infer global product triviality from fibrewise isomorphism without the bundle and base hypotheses.', 'Claim an executable global trivialization from the existence proof.']
- **limit-holomorphic-convexity-versus-steinness** (edge_case): The classified cover is holomorphically convex, but it is Stein precisely when its compact projective factor is a point. Blocked use: ['Conclude that every semialgebraically presented projective universal cover is Stein.']
- **limit-singer-hypotheses** (hypothesis): The stated reduced square-integrable cohomology vanishing requires a smooth connected closed aspherical projective source with the specified cover presentation. Total vanishing additionally requires a positive-dimensional affine factor. Blocked use: ['Transfer Singer vanishing to arbitrary normal projective varieties without asphericity.', 'Infer total vanishing when the affine factor is absent.']
- **limit-quasiprojective-refinement** (formal_scope): The quasi-projective-cover refinement uses external log abundance. Its finite quotient cover is a holomorphic bundle over an abelian variety and need not be a product bundle; the general-cover assertion gives only the stated pullback direction. Blocked use: ['Infer a product decomposition of the finite projective quotient cover.', 'Reverse the general-cover pullback assertion without additional proof.', "Omit the external abundance premise from the refinement's dependency record."]
- **limit-affine-uniformization-smoothness** (hypothesis): The affine-space uniformization corollary requires a connected smooth projective source whose ordinary universal cover is affine space of the corresponding complex dimension. Blocked use: ['Apply the abelian finite-cover conclusion to arbitrary nonprojective or singular spaces solely from an affine-space covering presentation.']

          "exact_quote": "Under the hypotheses of condition <span class=\"upright\">(i)</span> of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a> and the boundary construction of Theorem <a href=\"#win:construction\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"win:construction\">32</a>, there are a contractible Stein manifold $M$, biholomorphic to the boundedly realizable tower $P$, an equivariant surjective submersion $f:V\\to M$, and an equivariant holomorphic map $f_X:\\widetilde X\\to M$ with $f=f_X\\mu$. Then the descended proper map $X\\to M/\\Lambda$, its pullback to $M$, and every connected finite covering family of that pullback are flat and topologically locally trivial, with connected normal projective fibres.           "anchor": "stmt-cor-quasiprojective",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X$ be a connected normal projective variety over $\\mathbb C$, with ordinary universal cover $U$. The following are equivalent:*\n\n1.  *$U$ is biholomorphic to a quasi-projective variety.*\n\n2.  *There are a finite étale Galois cover $X'\\to X$, an abelian variety $A$, and a morphism $\\alpha:X'\\to A$ that is a locally trivial holomorphic fibre bundle with simply connected projective fibre.*\n\n3.  *For some integer $m\\geq0$ and some simply connected projective variety $F$, there is a biholomorphism $$U\\simeq\\mathbb C^m\\times F.$$*\n\n*A point is allowed as the abelian variety or the fibre.*\n\n*More generally, let $V\\to X$ be a connected infinite étale Galois covering of complex spaces, with $V$ biholomorphic to a quasi-projective variety.           "exact_quote": "<summary>Proof of stmt-cor-quasiprojective</summary>\n\n*Proof.* For every smooth projective complex variety $Y$ with nef $K_Y$, the pair $(Y,0)$ satisfies the hypotheses of (OpenAI 2026a, Theorem 1.1) [[OpenAILogAbundance2026](#ref-OpenAILogAbundance2026)]. <a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the Kollár–Pardon conjecture: the universal cover of a connected normal projective complex variety is biholomorphic to a semialgebraic open subset of a projective variety if and only if it is a product of a bounded symmetric domain, a complex affine space, and a simply connected normal projective variety.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The universal cover of a projective variety remembers its fundamental group through the deck action and its complex geometry through the space on which that group acts. In complex dimension one, uniformization leaves only the projective line, the complex line and the unit disc. The question considered here asks whether an analogous decomposition survives in arbitrary dimension when the universal cover admits a finite real-algebraic description.

A subset of a complex algebraic variety is *semialgebraic* if, in algebraic affine charts, it is defined by finite Boolean combinations of real polynomial equalities and inequalities in the real and imaginary coordinates. We say that a complex space has a *semialgebraic projective-open presentation* if it is biholomorphic to a semialgebraic open subset of a projective variety, with openness taken in the ordinary complex topology. This is a condition on the complex space: the biholomorphism is not required to be algebraic, and the deck transformations of a covering are not required to preserve the chosen semialgebraic structure.

A *bounded symmetric domain* is a connected bounded domain in a complex vector space for which every point is an isolated fixed point of a holomorphic involution of the domain. We allow a point as a zero-dimensional bounded symmetric domain. The compact factor in our result may be singular. All universal covers and fundamental groups below are taken in the classical topology.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (The Kollár–Pardon classification). *Let $X$ be a connected normal projective variety over $\mathbb C$, and let $\widetilde X$ be its universal cover. The following conditions are equivalent:*

1.  *$\widetilde X$ is biholomorphic to a semialgebraic open subset of a projective variety.*

2.  *There are an integer $m\geq0$, a bounded symmetric domain $D$, and a simply connected normal projective variety $F$ such that $$\widetilde X\simeq D\times\mathbb C^m\times F.$$*

*A point is allowed as either $D$ or $F$. No algebraicity of the deck transformations is assumed.*

</div>

This proves Conjecture 2 of Kollár and Pardon (Kollár and Pardon 2012, Conjecture 2) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[KollarPardon2012](#ref-KollarPardon2012)] in its normal-projective scope. The three factors have different roles. The bounded factor records hyperbolic transverse geometry, the affine factor arises from Albanese varieties of compact fibres after finite covers, and the remaining projective factor is simply connected. Retaining normal singularities is essential: the assertion does not replace the compact factor by a smooth resolution.

The group-theoretic input is the theorem of OpenAI that the entire ordinary fundamental group of a connected smooth special compact Kähler manifold is virtually abelian, meaning that it contains an abelian subgroup of finite index (OpenAI 2026b, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[SpecialAbelianity](#ref-SpecialAbelianity)]. We state its precise interface in Section <a href="#grp:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="grp:section">6</a> and apply it only to smooth compact special manifolds furnished by Campana’s core. That theorem is proved in the separately cited companion article. The geometric construction of the transverse quotient and the arguments passing from those compact fundamental groups to the deck action are proved here.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and related results

The affine-space case belongs to Iitaka’s uniformization problem: one asks whether a smooth projective variety covered by $\mathbb C^n$ is a finite unramified quotient of an abelian variety. Nakayama proved the relevant structure theorem under semiampleness of the canonical divisor and derived the affine-space statement from abundance (Nakayama 1999, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Nakayama1999](#ref-Nakayama1999)]. This is an important predecessor of the separation between a group-theoretic reduction and the geometry of the Albanese map.

Claudon, Höring and Kollár extended the question from affine to quasi-projective universal covers. Their classification and its general-cover extension were proved assuming smooth abundance (Claudon, Höring, and Kollár 2013, Theorem 1.1, Conjecture 1.2 and Corollary 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[ClaudonHoeringKollar2013](#ref-ClaudonHoeringKollar2013)]. The zero-boundary case of OpenAI’s log-abundance theorem (OpenAI 2026a, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[OpenAILogAbundance2026](#ref-OpenAILogAbundance2026)] supplies exactly that premise. Their Albanese bundle theorem itself was unconditional (Claudon, Höring, and Kollár 2013, Theorem 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[ClaudonHoeringKollar2013](#ref-ClaudonHoeringKollar2013)]. Semialgebraic openness admits the additional bounded symmetric factor, and Kollár and Pardon formulated the resulting product conjecture in (Kollár and Pardon 2012, Conjecture 2) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[KollarPardon2012](#ref-KollarPardon2012)].

Kollár and Pardon established a nonabelian lattice case of this conjecture. Let $X$ be smooth and projective, and let $\pi_1(X)\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma$ be a quotient such that $\Gamma$ acts freely, properly discontinuously and cocompactly on an irreducible bounded symmetric domain $D$ of dimension at least two. Their Theorem 3 identifies a biholomorphic realization of the associated $\Gamma$-cover as a semialgebraic subset of a projective variety with a fibre-bundle structure $X\to D/\Gamma$ and a product presentation of that cover as $D\times F$, with $F$ projective (Kollár and Pardon 2012, Theorem 3) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[KollarPardon2012](#ref-KollarPardon2012)]. Their proposed approach separates determining the fundamental group, constructing a morphism to the locally symmetric quotient, and proving the bundle assertion (Kollár and Pardon 2012, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[KollarPardon2012](#ref-KollarPardon2012)]. In the present proof, the intrinsic quotient is constructed before its deck action is proved discrete and its base is identified as symmetric.

Kollár and Pardon also proved a fibre-bundle theorem that remains an essential input here: if a morphism from a normal projective variety to a smooth projective variety with contractible universal cover has an entire semialgebraic projective-open pullback, then it is a holomorphic fibre bundle (Kollár and Pardon 2012, arXiv version 2, Theorem 20) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[KollarPardon2012](#ref-KollarPardon2012)]. Their topological and Chow-space arguments in Sections 1–2, together with the control of invariant subvarieties in (Claudon, Höring, and Kollár 2013, sec. 2) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[ClaudonHoeringKollar2013](#ref-ClaudonHoeringKollar2013)], are predecessors of our compact-factor argument. We use the bundle theorem after constructing the normal relative Albanese family, and adapt the invariant-subset method to the polarized fibre classes on its Stein universal base.

Compactifiable Kähler covers give a related analytic problem. Claudon and Höring, with Campana’s appendix, proved an Albanese bundle theorem after finite cover under a Kähler compactification and an almost abelian group hypothesis (Claudon and Höring 2013, Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[ClaudonHoering2013](#ref-ClaudonHoering2013)]. Their minimal-counterexample analysis identifies specialness under the weaker compactification condition (Claudon and Höring 2013, Proposition 1.4) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[ClaudonHoering2013](#ref-ClaudonHoering2013)]. Campana’s core (Campana 2004) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[Campana2004](#ref-Campana2004)] and the compact special abelianity theorem (OpenAI 2026b) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[SpecialAbelianity](#ref-SpecialAbelianity)] provide the group input in the present proof. The compactification conditions in these analytic results and semialgebraic projective openness are kept distinct.

A recent approach imposes definability on geometric data as well as on the space. Rogov proves a metric form of the Kollár–Pardon statement for smooth aspherical projective varieties with definable lifted Kähler metric (Rogov 2024, Theorem C) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[Rogov2024](#ref-Rogov2024)]. That metric hypothesis supplies information beyond a semialgebraic presentation of the complex space. A different recent result shows that finite-CW homotopy type alone does not suffice for the bundle conclusion: Corrêa, Kollár, Schreieder and Wang construct examples with finite-CW-type universal covers whose Albanese maps are not differentiable fibre bundles (Corrêa et al. 2026, Corollary 30) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[CorreaKollarSchreiederWang2026](#ref-CorreaKollarSchreiederWang2026)]. Our application of the Kollár–Pardon bundle theorem retains the entire semialgebraic pullback.

The analytic tools also have distinct roles. Real analytic uniformization and preparation (Bierstone and Milman 1988; Lion and Rolin 1997) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[BierstoneMilman1988](#ref-BierstoneMilman1988), [LionRolin1997](#ref-LionRolin1997)] supply controlled paths and finite scaling data. The thin-set theorem of Vâjâitu (Vâjâitu 2000) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[Vajaitu2000](#ref-Vajaitu2000)] turns small pseudoconcave omissions into analytic holes. Bochner’s tube theorem and Berndtsson’s variation theorem (Noguchi 2020; Berndtsson 2006) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[Noguchi2020](#ref-Noguchi2020), [Berndtsson2006](#ref-Berndtsson2006)] identify whole quadratic fibres. The Nadel–Frankel splitting theorem, in the form recalled by Farb–Weinberger (Farb and Weinberger 2008, Theorem 1.10) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[FarbWeinberger2008](#ref-FarbWeinberger2008)], identifies the transverse symmetric factor once the compact quotient has been constructed. Finally, Grauert’s Oka principle (Grauert 1958; Forstnerič and Prezelj 2000) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Grauert1958](#ref-Grauert1958), [ForstnericPrezelj2000](#ref-ForstnericPrezelj2000)] turns the resulting local compact-factor products over a contractible Stein base into a global product. The new constructions join these inputs through projective windows, bounded-path localization and an intrinsic quotient.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The proof and its main obstacles

We first explain the two geometric problems that prevent a direct application of bounded-domain uniformization. The given cover can contain compact positive-dimensional subvarieties, and its semialgebraic boundary can have several successive degeneracies. A scaling near one boundary face therefore need not produce the whole cover or a bounded domain. Moreover, convergence on compact subsets of a prospective limit gives only an interior inclusion: paths in the original domains could reach other components or escape through parts of the boundary lost under scaling. The proof constructs the compact directions and controls this additional boundary behaviour together.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
###### Successive boundary faces.

Write $U=\widetilde X$. A functorial resolution of its projective presentation gives a smooth semialgebraic open set $V$ and a proper modification $\mu:V\to U$. The same deck group acts on $V$, with smooth projective quotient. Compact Kähler extension makes $V$ locally pseudoconvex. Near a regular real-algebraic boundary hypersurface, the directions on which the Levi form vanishes integrate into complex plaques. Their algebraic closures supply projective parameter families. Scaling in the directions transverse to a plaque produces a quadratic epigraph whose coefficients are meromorphic functions on the plaque’s projective model. Repeating on that lower-dimensional model terminates in a finite tower of positive-matrix inequalities. We denote this limit domain by $P$.

Two kinds of retention are needed in this induction. Projective hole propagation ensures that each selected fibre is a whole projective variety minus an analytic subset, and that these holes stay inside a divisor defined over a full parameter box. Triangular recentering maps control the parameters along bounded paths. Analytic discs attached to their real orbits show that the endpoint of every such limiting path lies in $\overline P$. Sections <a href="#prep:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:section">2</a> and <a href="#loc:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="loc:section">3</a> establish these two tools; Section <a href="#win:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="win:section">4</a> applies them to the successive faces. The outcome includes contractibility and a bounded realization of $P$, together with complete real holomorphic vector fields that span its complex tangent spaces.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
###### From a window to an intrinsic quotient.

The projective families initially describe only local regions of $V$. For such a region, a bounded plurisubharmonic trace of its incident parameters is constant on every projective variety minus analytic holes. Strict plurisubharmonicity in the parameter then forces two intersecting fibres to coincide. Thus the fibres are intrinsic to $V$, even though their construction used a chosen boundary face. Holomorphic functions that decay at the moving chart boundaries control the exhaustion obtained by deck translations. We call these functions *guards*. They produce a global quotient $f:V\to M$, and a separate compactness argument for the inverse charts proves $M\simeq P$. This inverse argument is what upgrades limit maps to a biholomorphism.

The bounded realization makes $f$ constant on the compact fibres of $\mu$, so it descends to $f_X:U\to M$. To retain singular normal compact factors, we also prepare normal fibres, flatness, fibrewise resolutions and Whitney-stratified submersions before choosing the scales. The exhaustive translated charts propagate these properties to every fibre. Section <a href="#quo:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="quo:section">5</a> proves the quotient and these normal-source assertions.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
###### The transverse action and its kernel.

Put $\Gamma=\pi_1(X)$, let $\Lambda$ be its image in $\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(M)$, and put $K=\ker(\Gamma\to\Lambda)$. The induced action on $M$ is not initially known to be discrete. Campana’s core has smooth compact special general fibres. The abelianity theorem and a Liouville argument show that the lifts of each such fibre have countable image in $M$. Orbifold extension to the full proper projective parameter families turns these countable sets into locally finite sets. Their dense union forces the transverse action to be discrete. After a finite cover the action is free. The compact quotient has ample canonical bundle, and the Nadel–Frankel splitting theorem, combined with the spanning vector fields, identifies $M$ as a bounded symmetric domain. A compact-fibre monodromy map proves that $K$ is finitely generated; a second application of core extension and compact special abelianity proves that it is virtually abelian. These are the steps of Section <a href="#grp:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="grp:section">6</a>; transverse symmetry is derived within this argument.

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
###### Separating the affine and compact factors.

Choose a characteristic finite-index free-abelian subgroup $K'\subset K$. The normal projective fibres of $U/K'\to M$ have fundamental group $K'$. Their relative Albanese construction, carried out through the simultaneous resolution and descended by normality, gives a family of tori. The semialgebraic fibre-bundle theorem of Kollár–Pardon applies to the *entire* Albanese pullback. Its universal covering produces a proper map $$j:U\longrightarrow T\simeq M\times\mathbb C^m$$ with simply connected normal projective fibres.

The remaining issue is variation of those compact fibres. In a projective Chow space the fibres, and each of their polarized isomorphism classes, form semialgebraic loci. A topological rigidity argument forces the closure of every invariant class to be all of $T$. Two disjoint semialgebraic subsets cannot both be dense, so there is only one class. Relative Hilbert embeddings give local holomorphic products, and the Oka principle over the contractible Stein base $T$ gives the global product with $F$. Section <a href="#cmp:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cmp:section">7</a> proves this splitting and the converse. Section <a href="#sec:assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:assembly">8</a> assembles the classification. Section <a href="#sec:quasiprojective" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:quasiprojective">9</a> records the quasi-projective-cover consequences of smooth abundance. Figure <a href="#fig:classification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:classification">1</a> records the three maps constructed in the proof and the distinct group inputs.

<figure id="fig:classification">

<figcaption>The geometric maps in the proof. The top row constructs the intrinsic quotient on a resolution and descends it to the normal cover. The bottom row separates its affine and compact directions. Solid arrows are geometric maps; the dashed arrow records the theorem identifying the same base as symmetric. After the quotient is constructed, compact special abelianity is used both to prove transverse discreteness and to control the kernel $K$ needed for $j$.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Conventions

All projective varieties are reduced and irreducible unless stated otherwise; a connected normal variety has one irreducible component. A domain is connected and open. Local pseudoconvexity means local Steinness at the boundary in the indicated ambient complex manifold. Families with projective fibres may be analytic pullbacks of algebraic families; bounded algebraic data in their projective variables are specified when needed. Generic real parameters are chosen on relatively compact boxes after removing the exceptional subanalytic sets of the particular construction. The precise convention, including countable families of tests, is Definition <a href="#prep:generic" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:generic">2</a>.

<a id="prep:section"></a>
### Preparation, kernels, and projective holes

This section supplies the two kinds of control needed for projective windows. Preparation makes the boundary limits finite and preserves their real parameters. The second half of the section proves that projective fibres which are present up to algebraic holes retain this property throughout a connected parameter domain.

All manifolds in this section are complex manifolds unless real coordinates are specified. A locally pseudoconvex open subset of a manifold means an open subset which is locally Stein at every boundary point. The open sets are allowed to be disconnected before a distinguished component is selected. Subanalytic data on a relatively compact coordinate box are always assumed to extend as globally subanalytic data to a slightly larger box. Rational powers of a positive scale variable are allowed. Polynomial descriptions in projective variables are understood in a finite collection of bounded algebraic charts, including charts at infinity. In this paper, *bounded algebraic data* means that one such finite atlas and uniform finite bounds for the number and degrees of the polynomial fibre equations and tests have been fixed. The coefficient arrays depend analytically, or globally subanalytically, on the retained parameter boxes as specified in each statement. For an algebraic map, the same convention is imposed on its graph. A *bounded-data semialgebraic family* has a fixed finite Boolean description of this kind in real and imaginary fibre coordinates. These are bounds on finite algebraic complexity; estimates for coefficients, scale matrices or their inverses are stated separately when required.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### The preparation conventions

<a id="stmt-prep-generic"></a>
<div id="prep:generic" class="definition">

**Definition 2** (Generic real parameters). An assertion at generic parameters in an open real box means that it holds outside a locally finite union of subanalytic subsets of smaller dimension. When countably many fixed compact boxes or positive accuracies are tested, a countable union of such exceptional sets is permitted. Each application involving finitely many data is subsequently restricted to a neighborhood of the chosen parameter on which those data have the asserted regularity. No uniform neighborhood for all members of a countable list is implicit.

</div>

We use the following standard forms of real analytic preparation. The underlying results are the uniformization and rectilinearization theorems and the distance inequality of (Bierstone and Milman 1988, Theorems 0.1, 0.2, and 6.4) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[BierstoneMilman1988](#ref-BierstoneMilman1988)], together with one-variable preparation with parameters (Lion and Rolin 1997, Theorem 1 and Sections 0.3–0.4) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[LionRolin1997](#ref-LionRolin1997)]. Parusiński’s earlier preparation theorem (Parusiński 1994, Theorem 7.5) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Parusinski1994](#ref-Parusinski1994)] is a predecessor of this method; the specified centre condition used below is the one in Lion–Rolin. For finite definable partitions, choice and components we use (Coste 1999, Theorems 2.10, 3.1 and 3.9) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[Coste1999](#ref-Coste1999)].

<a id="stmt-prep-puiseux"></a>
<div id="prep:puiseux" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Power bounds and generic Puiseux preparation). *Let $A$ be globally subanalytic and let $f(v,t)$ be globally subanalytic for $v\in A$ and $0<t<\epsilon(v)$, where $\epsilon$ is positive. If $f(v,t)>0$, there is an integer $N$ such that, for every fixed $v$, $$t^N<f(v,t)<t^{-N}
 \quad\hbox{for all sufficiently small }t>0.$$ The threshold may depend on $v$. For finitely many such functions one may use a common $N$.*

*If $A$ is an open real box, then near a generic $v_0$ a given finite collection of functions admits convergent expansions $$f(v,t)=\sum_{j\geq j_0}a_j(v)t^{j/q}.$$ with a common positive integer $q$ and coefficients real analytic in $v$. After multiplication by a sufficiently high power of $t$, these are jointly real analytic functions of $(v,t^{1/q})$ through $t=0$. Convergence and every fixed number of $v$-derivatives are uniform on smaller compact parameter boxes.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prep-puiseux"><summary>Proof of stmt-prep-puiseux</summary>

*Proof.* Apply one-variable preparation with $t$ as the last variable. The finitely many preparation cylinders meeting $t=0$ have, on their bottom bands, center zero: a nonzero center comparable to $t$ cannot remain comparable as $t$ tends to zero with $v$ fixed. On each such band the prepared function is a rational power of $t$ times a nonzero function of $v$ times a unit bounded above and below. There are only finitely many rational exponents. Taking $N$ larger than all their absolute values absorbs the positive factors depending on fixed $v$ and proves the first assertion. Apply the same argument to $1/f$ when necessary.

For the second assertion, restrict to a full-dimensional base cell on which the finitely many coefficient functions in the preparation are analytic and their required nonzero denominators remain nonzero. In the analytic unit choose projective coordinate charts about the limiting arguments. An argument involving a negative power of $t$ either has identically zero numerator on the chosen base cell or tends to infinity; in the latter case its reciprocal is a valid analytic coordinate and its nonzero coefficient can be inverted. In these charts the arguments are analytic in $v$ and nonnegative rational powers of $t$. Clear their denominators. The unit is analytic on a neighborhood of its compact argument set, giving the asserted joint analytic extension. Ordinary convergence of analytic power series on a smaller box gives the derivative assertion. The discarded base cells and coefficient singularities have smaller dimension. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prep-fixed-parameters"></a>
<div id="prep:fixed-parameters" class="remark">

*Remark 4*. An estimate obtained with additional parameters held fixed does not permit those parameters to move during a correction. When a distance inequality is needed fibrewise, apply preparation to the definable fibrewise distance and residual functions. This gives a common power on finitely many parameter pieces; its positive constants and thresholds may still depend on the fixed parameters. Joint analytic preparation, when needed, requires restriction to a full-dimensional parameter piece.

Here is an explicit way to retain the fixed parameter. For compact closed fibres $C_v$ and a nonnegative continuous definable residual $r(v,z)$ whose zero set is $C_v$, put $$m(v,s)=\min\{r(v,z):\operatorname{dist}(z,C_v)\geq s\}$$ on a compact test box, assigning the value $1$ when the set is empty. This is positive for $s>0$. Lemma <a href="#prep:puiseux" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prep:puiseux">3</a> bounds it below by $s^N$ for sufficiently small $s$, with $N$ independent of fixed $v$. Compactness excludes points of fixed positive distance when their residual tends to zero. Consequently, for small residuals, $$\operatorname{dist}(z,C_v)\leq r(v,z)^{1/N}.$$ All corrections therefore take place in the same fibre $C_v$. Empty $C_v$ cause no spurious limit, since the residual then has a positive minimum on the compact test box.

</div>

<a id="stmt-prep-projective-models"></a>
<div id="prep:projective-models" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5** (Projective models with parameters). *Suppose an algebraic family in projective coordinates is pulled back by a local analytic parameter map. Suppose an irreducible member is specified by a marked irreducible germ. Near a generic point of a maximally totally real open parameter box, one may track that component on a local branch and replace the family and its evaluation maps by a smooth proper projective family of connected smooth models. The evaluation maps are holomorphic, and their restrictions to the projective fibres are algebraic of bounded data. Real analytic parameter dependence can first be complexified.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prep-projective-models"><summary>Proof of stmt-prep-projective-models</summary>

*Proof.* Track irreducible components on a relative Hilbert or Chow parameter space; for their algebraic existence and projectivity see (Grothendieck 1960--1961, sec. 3, Theorems 3.1–3.2) <a id="pm-cite-27"></a>[[Grothendieck1961](#ref-Grothendieck1961)] and (Andreotti and Norguet 1967, sec. 2.4(b), Proposition 4) <a id="pm-cite-28"></a>[[AndreottiNorguet1967](#ref-AndreottiNorguet1967)]. On the locus of a fixed component type the tracking map is generically finite. Remove its branch and nonflat loci and choose a local branch. Resolve the resulting total family and the graphs of the finitely many evaluation maps by projective modifications. Generic smoothness removes a further proper analytic subset of the complex parameter space. A maximally totally real open box is not contained in a proper complex analytic subset, by the holomorphic identity theorem. Thus these deletions are legitimate at generic real parameters. The selected generic fibres are irreducible, and their smooth models are connected. After shrinking, properness and smoothness preserve connectedness. All constructions are made with finitely many projective equations and modifications; restricting to a relatively compact parameter box supplies the stated bounded data. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Open kernels and finite jets

<a id="stmt-prep-kernel"></a>
<div id="prep:kernel" class="definition">

**Definition 6** (Open kernel). Let $U_t$ be open subsets of a fixed manifold $M$, $0<t<t_0$. Their open kernel is $$\mathcal K(U_t)=\{z\in M: \text{some neighborhood }W\ni z
       \text{ satisfies }W\subset U_t
       \text{ for every sufficiently small }t\}.$$ Equivalently, if $F_t=M\setminus U_t$, then $$\mathcal K(U_t)=M\setminus\limsup_{t\downarrow0}F_t,$$ where the upper limit allows both $t$ and the point of $F_t$ to vary. In a varying family these tests are relative to the total space; smooth local trivializations identify the limiting fibre. A marked kernel is the connected component containing a specified point of the open kernel.

</div>

The neighborhood quantifier in this definition is essential. In particular, pointwise eventual membership is insufficient. The compact subsets of an open kernel are eventually contained in $U_t$, with a neighborhood, by a finite covering argument.

We use the Hartogs-figure criterion for local pseudoconvexity and the Euclidean Levi theorem in their standard forms; see (Fritzsche and Grauert 2002, 213:II.3.4 and II.3.7) <a id="pm-cite-29"></a>[[FritzscheGrauert2002](#ref-FritzscheGrauert2002)] and (Demailly 2012, VIII.9.11(a)) <a id="pm-cite-30"></a>[[Demailly2012](#ref-Demailly2012)]. The exhaustion and directional-radius criteria used below are (Demailly 2012, I.7.2) <a id="pm-cite-31"></a>[[Demailly2012](#ref-Demailly2012)].

<a id="stmt-prep-kernel-pseudoconvexity"></a>
<div id="prep:kernel-pseudoconvexity" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (Pseudoconvexity of open kernels). *An open kernel of locally pseudoconvex open sets is locally pseudoconvex. The assertion also holds in varying manifolds equipped with smoothly converging holomorphic coordinate charts.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prep-kernel-pseudoconvexity"><summary>Proof of stmt-prep-kernel-pseudoconvexity</summary>

*Proof.* Use the local Hartogs-figure criterion in a coordinate polydisc. A relatively compact Hartogs figure with a neighborhood contained in the kernel is contained, together with that neighborhood, in every $U_t$ for sufficiently small $t$. Pseudoconvexity fills its associated polydisc in each $U_t$. To obtain neighborhood inclusion for a compact subset of the filling, slightly enlarge the figure within the given neighborhood before applying the criterion.
